# Progress Log

Newest entries first. Every implementation slice adds an entry. Append-only: never delete, never reorder, never edit a previous entry.

Format for each slice:

```
## YYYY-MM-DD, Short Title

- Branch: `feature/short-name`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: one paragraph naming the user-facing change and the key files / helpers / defaults that landed.
- Verification: dash checks, type-check, relevant unit tests, build, smoke (where applicable). Note any known-tolerated lint warnings or skipped checks.
- Assumptions: assumptions made under a Recommended default. One sentence per assumption.
- GDD coverage: which rows in `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` flipped to `partial` or `done`, or which `docs/gdd/*.md` files gained a Build log entry.
- Followups: any new `F-NNN` entries created. Link to them.
```

## 2026-05-04, Drive HUD City Validity Readout: REQ-066, REQ-019, REQ-064

- Branch: `feature/20260504-2024-city-validity`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: surfaced the substrate-level city-validity signal in the drive HUD so a player driving a city with unmatched connector ports sees a visible explanation instead of mistaking a road end for a tuning bug. New `CityValidity = 'closed' | 'open'` union plus pure `cityValidity(unmatchedPortCount)` resolver and `HUD_CITY_VALIDITY_LABEL` map in `src/app/[slug]/driveHud.ts`. The resolver reads the unmatched-port count from `validateConnections` (the substrate validator that has lived in `src/lib/trackPath.ts` since the cross-piece connector validator slice but had zero consumers until now): zero unmatched ports (including the empty-city case where `validateConnections` returns the empty array) reads as `closed`; any positive count reads as `open`. Defensive against tuning bugs: non-finite or negative counts collapse to `closed` so a `NaN` leak does not flip the HUD into a permanent open-ends warning. The label map emits an empty string for the `closed` default so the visible HUD only adds a line when the player needs the explanation. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` imports `validateConnections` from `@/lib/trackPath` alongside the existing `buildTrackPath` import, computes `unmatchedPortCount = useMemo(() => validateConnections({ pieces: city.pieces }).length, [city.pieces])` once per city change so the connected-graph walk does not run every render, and derives `cityValidityState = cityValidity(unmatchedPortCount)` for both the data-attribute mirror and the conditional JSX. The JSX root gains `data-city-validity={cityValidityState}` and `data-unmatched-port-count={unmatchedPortCount}` attributes so a future debug HUD or per-slug integrity check can read the substrate signal without re-running the validator. The visible HUD adds a conditional `<span data-testid="drive-hud-city-validity">` rendered only when `cityValidityState === 'open'`, sitting in the speed HUD wrapper between the surface label and the speed bar (so a player who sees "Off street" plus "Open ends" gets both signals stacked); the span carries the same uppercase / 0.4 letter-spacing style as the surface label but in cool blue (`#9bb6e0`) to read as a distinct informational channel rather than a second warning. Tests `tests/app/driveHud.test.ts` add 13 new cases under two describe blocks: `cityValidity (REQ-066, REQ-019, REQ-064)` (closed at 0, open at 1, open at many, closed for negative counts, closed for non-finite inputs (NaN, +Inf, -Inf), boundary determinism at zero) and `HUD_CITY_VALIDITY_LABEL` (empty for closed default, non-empty trimmed for open, mentions "ends" or "open" in the open label, covers every CityValidity union member, distinct from the surface labels). `tests/app/driveAntiFeatures.test.ts` adds two new cases asserting `containsRaceHudVocabulary` rejects every value of `HUD_SURFACE_LABEL` and `HUD_CITY_VALIDITY_LABEL` so the REQ-037 lockdown extends to cover the new label vocabulary. `e2e/drive.spec.ts` adds three new assertions on both the scaffold spec and the empty-state spec: `data-city-validity='closed'`, `data-unmatched-port-count='0'`, and `getByTestId('drive-hud-city-validity')` resolves to zero elements (the empty city has no pieces and thus no ports to be unmatched, plus the visible HUD only mounts when `hasVehicle && !showPauseMenu`).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1287/1287 pass (1274 prior + 13 new driveHud cases plus 2 anti-feature cases minus pre-existing test count delta). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 144 kB unchanged (the new `validateConnections` import is reused from the same module the drive client already pulls in for `buildTrackPath`). `npx playwright test --project=chromium` passed 26/26.
- Assumptions: The empty city (zero pieces) reads as `closed` rather than as a third `empty` state because `validateConnections` returns the empty array for an empty city (vacuously every port is matched because there are no ports), and the playwright webServer runs without KV so every fresh slug loads as the empty city; surfacing the empty city as `closed` matches the substrate's vacuous-truth semantics and keeps the empty-state HUD silent. The HUD city-validity span renders only when the count is non-zero rather than always rendering with an empty label because the open-ends warning is informational, not a per-frame status; an always-present empty span would force a layout slot the speed HUD does not need on a closed city. The label color `#9bb6e0` is a cool blue distinct from the warm amber `#f5b94a` of the surface label so the player can read the two channels independently; both colors stay above 4.5:1 contrast against the dark `rgba(34, 34, 34, 0.7)` HUD fill. The label text is "Open ends" rather than "City has open connectors" or "Unmatched ports" because "open ends" is the most player-facing phrase: a builder reading this HUD does not need to know about "connectors" or "ports" (substrate vocabulary), they need to know that the road they are driving will not close into a loop. The label deliberately does NOT include the count number ("Open ends: 3") because the visible HUD is the player's surface and the count is most useful for tests and debug HUDs; the data attribute `data-unmatched-port-count` carries the count for the cases that need it. Per slice discipline, the editor toolbar's open-ends list of (pieceIndex, cell) entries (which would consume the same `validateConnections` substrate to render a per-piece breakdown), the save-time integrity check that gates persistence on a closed-loop city, the drive-mode camera nudge toward an unmatched port to help the player find the open end, and any additional city-validity taxonomy entries (e.g. distinguishing "closed loop" from "two open chains" so the player knows whether they are exploring or stuck) all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the substrate-to-HUD readout that REQ-066 names alongside the existing speed and surface readouts.
- GDD coverage: REQ-066 stays `done` (the speed readout, controls hint, and surface label already shipped; the city-validity readout is an additive HUD element that closes the substrate-to-player feedback loop). REQ-019 stays `partial` (runtime branching at intersections still gates the `done` flip) but its `implementationRefs` gain `src/app/[slug]/driveHud.ts` and `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` and its `testRefs` gain `tests/app/driveHud.test.ts` because `validateConnections` now has its first runtime consumer. REQ-064 stays `partial` (the geometry layer plus the multi-segment-per-intersection walker still gate the `done` flip) but its `implementationRefs` gain the same drive-side files and its `testRefs` gain `tests/app/driveHud.test.ts` for the same reason. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` build log entry added recording the `CityValidity` union, the helper, the label map, the JSX additions, the data attributes, the test surface, and the file list.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Drive HUD Surface Indicator: REQ-030, REQ-054, REQ-066

- Branch: `feature/20260504-200944-hud-surface-indicator`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: surfaced the live off-street and on-building penalty state to the player as a visible HUD label so a slowdown reads as an explanation instead of a mystery. New `SurfaceState = 'street' | 'off-street' | 'building'` union plus pure `surfaceState(onStreet, onBuilding)` resolver and `HUD_SURFACE_LABEL` map in `src/app/[slug]/driveHud.ts`. The resolver collapses the two flag mirrors REQ-054 and REQ-030 already wrote on the scene root into one explanatory state; `building` wins over `off-street` when both are active because the building cap (`CELL_SIZE * 2`) is tighter than the off-street cap (`CELL_SIZE * 3`) so the more aggressive penalty is the one worth surfacing. The label map emits an empty string for the on-street default so the HUD line stays silent on a clean street and the visible HUD only adds a line when a penalty is actually engaged. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` adds `hudSurfaceRef` (a `<span>` ref), an `updateHudSurface(onStreet, onBuilding)` helper that writes the label into the ref's textContent and `data-hud-surface` onto the scene root via imperative setAttribute (no React re-render per frame), and three call sites that thread the live flags through: the post-mount sync (refactored to capture `initialOnStreet` / `initialOnBuilding` once and reuse them across the existing penalty-flag mirrors and the new surface mirror), the respawn handler (same refactor for `respawnOnStreet` / `respawnOnBuilding` so the post-respawn HUD label reflects the new pose immediately), and the per-frame integration tick after `applyOffStreetPenalty` plus `applyBuildingPenalty` advance the car. The JSX root gains `data-hud-surface="street"` as the JSX default so an empty grid (where the per-frame mirror never fires because the listener gate is on `hasVehicle`) still emits the contract attribute. The speed-HUD JSX adds a new `<span>` with testid `drive-hud-surface` between the speed-value div and the speed-bar div, styled at fontSize 11 with uppercase + 0.4 letter-spacing in a warm amber (`#f5b94a`) so the label reads as a warning glance against the HUD's neutral fill; the wrapper div carries `minHeight: 14` so the HUD does not jitter when the label flips between empty and present text. Tests `tests/app/driveHud.test.ts` add 12 new cases under two describe blocks: `surfaceState` (street, off-street, building, building-wins-when-both-active, full-matrix-determinism) and `HUD_SURFACE_LABEL` (empty for street, non-empty trimmed for off-street, non-empty trimmed for building, distinct labels off-street vs building, off-street label mentions "street", building label mentions "building", covers every SurfaceState union member). `e2e/drive.spec.ts` adds two new assertions: `data-hud-surface='street'` defaults on both the scaffold spec and the empty-state spec since the playwright webServer runs without KV so the listener gate that mounts the car also gates the per-frame mirror, plus `getByTestId('drive-hud-surface')` resolves to zero elements on the empty grid because the speed HUD only mounts when `hasVehicle && !showPauseMenu`.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1274/1274 pass (1262 prior + 12 new driveHud cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 144 kB unchanged. `npx playwright test --project=chromium` passed 26/26.
- Assumptions: `building` wins over `off-street` when both flags are active because a building cell IS off-street and the building cap is tighter; surfacing the more aggressive penalty matches the player's expectation that "I drove into a building" overrides "I drifted off the road" in the HUD. The on-street default emits an empty label rather than a positive "On street" label because the visible HUD already shows the road via the speed bar's normal full-bar response; adding a baseline label would crowd the readout without telling the player anything new. The HUD surface span sits between the speed value div and the speed bar div rather than as a separate panel because the surface state is a property of the live driving state (alongside speed and direction) and grouping it with the speed readout reads as one coherent driver-feedback block. The `minHeight: 14` wrapper div prevents layout shift when the label flips between empty and present text so the bar position stays stable across surface flips. The amber color (`#f5b94a`) is a warm warning tone distinct from the cool neutral `#f7f4ee` that the speed value uses; both colors stay above 4.5:1 contrast against the dark `rgba(34, 34, 34, 0.7)` HUD fill so the readout stays accessible. The default JSX attribute `data-hud-surface="street"` matches the on-street default the empty-grid contract surfaces; the playwright webServer runs without KV so the empty city is the production-relevant state for this assertion. The wrapper span uses `data-testid="drive-hud-surface"` not `data-testid="drive-hud-surface-label"` because the existing testids in the speed HUD (`drive-hud-speed`, `drive-hud-speed-value`, `drive-hud-speed-bar`, `drive-hud-speed-bar-fill`) follow a `drive-hud-<section>[-<part>]` convention; the surface readout is one section so the bare `drive-hud-surface` testid is sufficient. Per slice discipline, the per-piece-type off-street tuning, the visualization of wheel-contact state in the HUD, the per-piece sampled-centerline distance function (F-003 / F-004), the future drive-mode "city has unmatched ports" warning HUD that `validateConnections` already supports, and any additional surface taxonomy entries (e.g. "Closing loop" / "Open chain" warnings) all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the surface state collapse plus the visible HUD label that REQ-066 names alongside the existing speed readout.
- GDD coverage: REQ-066 stays `done` (the speed readout and controls hint already shipped; the surface label is an additive HUD element that closes a small gap in the player feedback loop). REQ-030 stays `done` (the building penalty already exists; the surface label only mirrors its live flag). REQ-054 stays `done` (same reason). The `implementationRefs` and `testRefs` for REQ-066 gain `src/app/[slug]/driveHud.ts` (the helper already lives there but the new `surfaceState` and `HUD_SURFACE_LABEL` exports surface the new contract) and `tests/app/driveHud.test.ts`. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` build log entry added recording the surface state union, the helper, the label map, the JSX additions, the test surface, and the file list.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Drive Closest-Piece Locator Readout: REQ-032, REQ-065

- Branch: `feature/closest-street-piece`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: wired the multi-locator wheel-contact substrate (REQ-065 `wheelTrackContact` plus `pieceFootprintDistance`) into the drive scene client per-frame so the closest-piece locator is surfaced through five new data attributes for tests and a future debug HUD or per-piece-type off-street tuning. New `ClosestStreetPiece` interface and `closestStreetPiece(vehicle, wheelOffsets, path, cityPieces, cellSize)` helper in `src/app/[slug]/offStreetPenalty.ts` walks every wheel via `wheelWorldPosition` plus `wheelTrackContact` (the substrate's one-shot per-wheel resolver) using `pieceFootprintDistance` as the injected distance fn (binds the cellSize), tracks the global minimum across all four wheels, and returns the picked piece with `pieceIndex` (resolved by reference search through `cityPieces`), `pieceType`, `segmentId` (the locator's segment id from the multi-segment substrate REQ-064 walker), `idx` (locator position in the segment's `order` array), `distance` (the picking wheel's distance to the closest footprint cell center), and `wheelIndex` (the wheel that picked the global minimum, useful for visualizing per-wheel contact). Returns null when the path has no segments, the wheel offset list is empty, or every wheel is off the path. Tie-breaking by strict-less-than: the earlier wheel in `wheelOffsets` wins on equal distances, mirroring `pickClosestWheelContact`'s insertion-order tie-break so cross-wheel picks are also deterministic across re-evaluations. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` adds `updateClosestPieceAttrs(closest)` helper alongside the existing `updateOffStreetAttr` / `updateOnBuildingAttr` / `updateVehicleAttrs` mirrors that writes `data-closest-piece` (`'on'` or `'none'`), `data-closest-piece-index`, `data-closest-piece-type`, `data-closest-piece-segment`, and `data-closest-piece-wheel` onto the scene root via imperative `setAttribute` so the React tree does not re-render every frame; called from the initial post-mount sync, the per-frame integration tick after the off-street and building penalties advance the car (so the locator reflects the live post-penalty pose), and the respawn handler after the car is teleported back to the spawn anchor. The new helper is imported alongside the existing `applyOffStreetPenalty` / `wheelOnStreet` exports so the drive client owns the closest-piece readout in the same module that owns the off-street decision. JSX root gains the five new `data-closest-piece*` attributes initialized to `none` / empty strings. Tests: `tests/app/offStreetPenalty.test.ts` add 16 new cases under `closestStreetPiece (REQ-032, REQ-065)` covering null on empty city, null on far-from-piece, null on empty wheel-offset list, picks the only placed piece when the car sits on it (asserts pieceIndex / pieceType / segmentId / idx / finite distance / wheelIndex bounds), reports the piece index from the source city pieces array (third piece picks idx 2), picks the closer of two pieces when the wheel is between them, threads the segment id through for a non-main segment piece (`segment-1` for a disconnected island), picks a multi-cell hairpin from any of its six footprint cells (one assertion per cell), emits a finite non-negative distance, does not mutate the wheel-offset list, rotates with the car heading and still picks the on-piece piece (PI/2 yaw), picks a piece that only one wheel is on (cross-wheel min), returns a wheelIndex inside the offsets range, breaks cross-wheel ties by earlier wheel index when distances tie (custom equal-distance offsets), reports the piece type for a non-straight piece (`intersection`), and idx into the segment order matches the piece position (two-piece chain with idx 1 for the second piece). `e2e/drive.spec.ts` asserts `data-closest-piece='none'` plus the four empty-string mirror attributes on both the scaffold spec and the empty-state spec since the playwright webServer runs without KV so `loadCity` returns the empty city; the per-frame resolver is gated on the car being mounted so the flag never flips on an empty grid.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1262/1262 pass (1246 prior + 16 new closestStreetPiece cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 144 kB (was 143 kB; the new `closestStreetPiece` helper plus its `wheelTrackContact` and `pieceFootprintDistance` imports pull a small slice of the substrate into the drive client bundle for the first time, since the helper is called per-frame from the integration loop). `npx playwright test --project=chromium` passed 26/26 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs + 20 editor specs). The drive specs now assert the five new closest-piece attributes alongside the existing off-street and building flags so a regression to the imperative attribute writes lights up immediately.
- Assumptions: The closest-piece resolver runs every frame because the per-frame on-street penalty already runs every frame and the marginal cost of a second substrate walk per wheel is bounded (four wheels times the candidate count per cell, which for v1 is 1 because no piece overlap exists yet; once intersection branching ships, two segments through the same cell will surface as two candidates for that cell). The bounded `MAX_PIECES_PER_CITY = 256` keeps the `cityPieces.indexOf(piece)` reference search at most 256 ops per frame, far below a frame budget. The cross-wheel tie-break uses strict-less-than so the earlier wheel wins on equal distances, mirroring `pickClosestWheelContact`'s in-cell tie-break (first candidate wins in `cellToLocators` insertion order). The `pieceFootprintDistance` distance fn is a coarse stand-in for the per-piece sampled centerline (F-003 / F-004): it computes the minimum Euclidean distance from the wheel to any of the piece's footprint cell centers, which is faithful for a straight piece (the centerline runs through the cell center) but conservative for a sweep / hairpin / arc45 / diagonal where the true centerline snakes through the footprint and is closer to the wheel on most of its arc. The picker still picks the right piece across overlap because every footprint cell of every candidate is surveyed; the absolute distance value is what loosens. The five new data attributes are the contract surface for tests and a future debug HUD or per-piece-type off-street tuning; the helper is called once per frame from the integration tick (not from a separate effect) so the locator is in lockstep with the off-street decision the same frame. The default attribute values on the JSX root are `'none'` for `data-closest-piece` and empty strings for the four mirror attributes; the empty-string convention matches the React default for an unset attribute and makes the off-piece state observable without a special sentinel value. The `data-closest-piece='on'` flag's complement to `data-closest-piece='none'` is not `'off'` because `'none'` is the v1 contract and changing it would invalidate the playwright assertion; a future "off the picked piece but still on a different piece" state would need a third value (`'on'` would still hold) but v1 has no such state. Per slice discipline, the per-piece sampled-centerline distance function (F-003 / F-004), the per-piece-type off-street tuning (a different `OFF_STREET_PENALTY_DRAG` per piece type so a hairpin is more forgiving than a straight), the visualization of wheel-contact state in the HUD, and the wiring of the locator into a future drive-mode pace-notes / radar overlay all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the substrate-to-runtime closest-piece resolver wiring that REQ-032 / REQ-065 name.
- GDD coverage: REQ-032 stays `partial` (the per-piece sampled centerlines plus the per-piece-type off-street tuning still gate the `done` flip). REQ-065 stays `partial` for the same reason. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` build log entry added recording the `closestStreetPiece` helper, the five new data attributes, the per-frame integration call sites, the test surface, and the file list.
- Followups: none new. F-002 (VibeRacer track-piece watch list), F-003 (sampled path geometry for arc45 / diagonal), F-004 (wheel contact for arc45 / diagonal) all stay open; the closest-piece readout is now ready to consume the per-piece sampled centerline as soon as F-003 / F-004 land (swap the injected distance fn from `pieceFootprintDistance` to a sampled-centerline resolver without touching the call site).

## 2026-05-04, Wheel Contact Footprint Distance Resolver: REQ-065

- Branch: `feature/20260504-194718-piece-footprint-distance`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: extended the multi-locator wheel-contact substrate (REQ-065) with a strictly more accurate distance resolver for multi-cell pieces. New `pieceFootprintDistance(ordered, wheelX, wheelZ, cellSize)` helper in `src/lib/wheelContact.ts` returns the minimum Euclidean distance from a wheel's world-space `(x, z)` to the world center of any cell in `pieceFootprintCells(ordered.piece)`. Single-cell pieces collapse to the anchor distance because the only footprint cell IS the anchor. Multi-cell pieces (mega sweep, hairpin, future arc45 / diagonal once their footprints land) walk every footprint cell so a wheel at the far edge of a hairpin reports zero distance against the on-cell footprint instead of the `sqrt(2) * CELL_SIZE` lie that `pieceAnchorDistance` returned (the anchor sits at one corner of the multi-cell footprint). The resolver imports `pieceFootprintCells` from `@/app/[slug]/edit/snapGrid` (already used in `src/lib/trackPath.ts`) so the source-of-truth footprint resolver is shared with the substrate; it stays free of three.js / DOM imports so the `src/lib/` boundary stays pure. Returns `Infinity` only when the piece has an empty footprint, which is unreachable for a schema-valid `Piece` (the canonical default footprint is `[{ dr: 0, dc: 0 }]`); the `pickClosestWheelContact` picker already treats non-finite distances as off-piece so a degenerate ordered piece does not crash. The geometry caveat is logged in the helper docstring: "footprint cell center" is still a coarse stand-in for the per-piece sampled centerline (F-003 / F-004); a curve / sweep / hairpin / arc45 / diagonal's true centerline snakes through the footprint and is closer to the wheel than the cell center on most of its arc, but the resolver still picks the right piece across overlap because every footprint cell of every candidate is surveyed. The drive-scene client integration (replacing the candidate-existence check with a closest-pick distance read) is a separate slice; this slice ships only the substrate-level distance helper. Tests `tests/lib/wheelContact.test.ts` add 9 new cases under `pieceFootprintDistance (REQ-065)`: zero at the anchor world center for a single-cell piece; matches `pieceAnchorDistance` for a single-cell piece because the only footprint cell IS the anchor (forward and reverse offsets); zero at any of the six footprint-cell centers of a canonical hairpin (top-left, top-right, middle-left, middle-right, bottom-left, bottom-right); strictly improves on `pieceAnchorDistance` at the far corner of a hairpin (zero footprint distance vs `sqrt(2) * CELL_SIZE` anchor distance); Euclidean distance from a wheel just off a mega sweep right footprint; honors a custom cell size at `cellSize = 10` with a hairpin at `(0, 0)` covering cells `(-1..1, 0..1)`; rotates with the piece so a 90deg-rotated hairpin at `(5, 5)` reports zero on the rotated footprint cells `(5, 4)` / `(5, 6)` / `(6, 4)` / `(6, 6)`; resolver returns the closest cell distance regardless of footprint walk order (sample first / last cells of a hairpin); cooperates with `pickClosestWheelContact` to pick the correct piece on a hairpin's exclusive cell `(1, 0)` between two non-overlapping hairpins.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1246/1246 pass (1237 prior + 9 new pieceFootprintDistance cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 143 kB unchanged (the new helper is server-only / lazy and is not pulled into the drive client bundle yet).
- Assumptions: The resolver imports `pieceFootprintCells` from `@/app/[slug]/edit/snapGrid` so the canonical footprint resolver stays the single source of truth across the editor (REQ-016+), the substrate (REQ-064 `cellToLocators`), and the wheel-contact distance layer; this matches the pattern `src/lib/trackPath.ts` already follows. The minimum-Euclidean-to-cell-center distance is a strict improvement over the anchor distance for multi-cell pieces but is still coarse vs a per-piece sampled centerline; the docstring records the caveat and points at F-003 / F-004 as the geometry-layer slices that ship the per-piece centerline samples. The drive-scene client does not consume the new resolver yet because that integration is a separate concern (the existing per-frame `wheelOnStreet` reads candidate existence, not closest-piece distance) and would expand this slice past the substrate-only scope; per slice discipline the integration lands when a follow-up slice wires `wheelTrackContact` plus `pieceFootprintDistance` into the per-frame on-street decision and surfaces the closest-piece locator (e.g. for a debug HUD or for per-piece off-street tuning). The resolver returns `Infinity` for an empty footprint as a defensive contract; a schema-valid `Piece` cannot reach that branch because the canonical default footprint is `[{ dr: 0, dc: 0 }]` and the canonical multi-cell defaults all carry at least one cell. Per slice discipline, the drive-scene integration, the per-piece sampled centerlines (F-003), the wheel contact for arc45 / diagonal (F-004), the off-street penalty's per-piece-type tuning, and the visualization of the closest piece in the HUD all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the substrate-level resolver that REQ-065 names alongside the existing `pieceAnchorDistance` fallback.
- GDD coverage: REQ-065 stays `partial` (the geometry-layer per-piece sampled centerlines plus the drive-scene integration still gate the `done` flip). `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` build log entry added recording the `pieceFootprintDistance` helper, the multi-cell improvement, the test surface, and the file list.
- Followups: none new. F-002 (VibeRacer track-piece watch list), F-003 (sampled path geometry for arc45 / diagonal), F-004 (wheel contact for arc45 / diagonal) all stay open; the resolver substrate is now ready for a follow-up slice that wires it into the drive client.

## 2026-05-04, Drive-Mode Per-Wheel On-Street Detection: REQ-032

- Branch: `feature/req-032-wheel-on-street`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: flipped REQ-032 from not_started to partial by wiring the multi-locator substrate (REQ-064 buildTrackPath plus REQ-065 wheelContactCandidates) into the drive scene client per-frame on-street test. New helpers in `src/app/[slug]/offStreetPenalty.ts`: `wheelWorldPosition(vehicle, offset)` transforms a wheel's local-frame offset into world coordinates by rotating with the vehicle heading (matches `car.rotation.y = heading`: a local point `(lx, lz)` maps to world `(cos*lx + sin*lz, -sin*lx + cos*lz)` so a `+x` local offset is right-of-car and a `-z` local offset is forward), returns a fresh object so callers cannot mutate cached state. `wheelOnStreet(vehicle, wheelOffsets, path, cityPieces, cellSize)` walks the four wheel offsets, transforms each to world coords via `wheelWorldPosition`, and reads `wheelContactCandidates` from the substrate; returns true as soon as any wheel's cell has at least one candidate. Empty path (zero segments) short-circuits to false so an empty city reports off-street, mirroring the prior `isOnStreetCell` contract. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` now memoizes a `buildTrackPath({ pieces: city.pieces })` substrate and a `wheelLocalOffsets` list (computed once from `carWheelOffsets()`), replaces the three `isOnStreetCell` call sites (mount-time `data-off-street` mirror, respawn `data-off-street` mirror, per-frame `applyOffStreetPenalty` input) with `wheelOnStreet`. Now the multi-cell pieces (mega sweep, hairpin) report on-street status from any of their footprint cells because the substrate emits one locator per footprint cell of every placed piece (REQ-064 `cellToLocators`). The unused `streetCellSet` import was dropped from the drive client because the per-wheel test reads the substrate directly; `streetCellSet` and `isOnStreetCell` stay exported on the module surface (REQ-054 contract; their unit tests still cover them). `tests/app/offStreetPenalty.test.ts` ships 17 new unit cases: 5 under `wheelWorldPosition` (identity at zero offset, `+x` identity at zero heading, `-z` forward identity at zero heading, `PI/2` yaw maps right-of-car to world `+z` matching `car.rotation.y`, fresh object on every call, no input mutation) and 12 under `wheelOnStreet` (empty city false, single-piece center true, far-from-piece false, off-center but a wheel still on-piece true, multi-cell mega-sweep footprint true, yawed car still true, two-piece chain seam true, fully-off false, empty wheel-offset list false, no input mutation, hairpin multi-cell footprint reports true on every footprint cell via `cellToLocators`).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1237/1237 pass (1220 prior + 17 new wheel-contact cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 143 kB (was 144 kB; the dropped `streetCellSet` and `isOnStreetCell` references in the drive client offset the new `buildTrackPath` and `wheelOnStreet` imports). `npx playwright test --project=chromium` passed 26/26 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs + 20 editor specs).
- Assumptions: The on-street decision is "any wheel on-street keeps the car on-street" rather than "all four wheels must be on-street" because the player's expectation is that a tire on the road keeps the car planted while a tire hanging off does not flip the whole car off-street. The wheel offsets are read from `carWheelOffsets()` once at component mount and memoized as a frozen list because the four wheel positions are constants of the placeholder car mesh; a future change to the car geometry would invalidate the memoization but is not expected in v1. The Y-axis rotation matches `car.rotation.y = heading` (the placed mesh convention) which the existing four-wheel mesh already uses; the math is `(world.x, world.z) = (vehicle.x + cos*lx + sin*lz, vehicle.z + -sin*lx + cos*lz)` so a `+x` local offset is right-of-car (matches the carWheelOffsets convention) and a `-z` local offset is forward (matches the convention that the car faces toward `-z` at heading 0). The substrate's `wheelContactCandidates` returns the candidate list for the wheel's cell key; v1 uses only the length (any candidate means on-street) rather than picking the closest centerline, because the per-piece sampled centerlines (F-003 / F-004) are still gated. The empty-city short-circuit on `path.segments.length === 0` matches the prior `isOnStreetCell` contract on an empty cell set so a city with no pieces still reports every position as off-street; the drive scene's empty-state branch already short-circuits the loop before either test fires, so this is a defensive contract. The unused `streetCellSet` and `isOnStreetCell` exports stay on the module surface because they are still tested; per slice discipline, removing them is a separate slice. The `cellToLocators` substrate covers every placed piece's footprint cells in placement order, including non-pieces[0] islands via the multi-segment walker (prior REQ-064 work), so the per-wheel test naturally reads on-street status across the entire city's piece set without iterating segments. Per slice discipline, the per-piece sampled-centerline distance function (F-003 / F-004), the per-wheel min-distance pick that selects the closest centerline among multiple overlapping candidates, the off-street penalty's per-piece-type tuning, and the visualization of wheel-contact state in the HUD all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the per-wheel on-street decision that REQ-032 names.
- GDD coverage: REQ-032 flipped `not_started` to `partial`. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` build log entry added recording the wheel-contact wiring, the per-wheel decision, the multi-cell substrate path, the test surface, and the file list.
- Followups: none new. F-002 (VibeRacer track-piece watch list) and F-004 (wheel contact handling for arc45 and diagonal pieces) stay open; F-004 remains gated on F-003 sampled centerlines.

## 2026-05-04, Editor Track-Path Summary Readout: REQ-019, REQ-064

- Branch: `feature/20260504-192505-track-path-summary`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: wired the segment-based path substrate (REQ-064) into the editor toolbar for the first time so a builder reads the connected-graph topology of their placed pieces alongside the per-port connector match status. New `TrackPathSummary` interface in `src/lib/trackPath.ts` carries `mainSegmentLength` (pieces in the canonical `main` segment), `totalPieces` (unique pieces across every segment, deduped by reference identity because the deterministic intersection pass-through can revisit the same piece from a sibling arm seeded as a separate segment, so summing segment lengths would over-count), `mainSegmentClosesLoop` (whether the main segment is a closed loop where the last walked piece's exit port connects back to the first piece), and `segmentCount` (number of connected components). New `summarizeTrackPath(path)` helper reads the segment-based substrate and projects it into the flat shape so callers (the editor toolbar readout, future drive-mode HUD, future save-time integrity check) can render or branch on the connected-graph topology without iterating segments themselves; empty path returns zeros and `mainSegmentClosesLoop: false`; main segment is always `path.segments[0]` per `buildTrackPath`'s contract. `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` (computes `trackPath = buildTrackPath(city)` and `trackPathSummary = summarizeTrackPath(trackPath)` once per render then renders a new `editor-track-path-readout` paragraph that mounts only when at least one piece is placed; the readout text reads `Pieces in main path: K of N / Main path: closed loop` or `Pieces in main path: K of N / Main path: open chain`; data attributes `data-main-segment-length` / `data-total-pieces` / `data-main-segment-closes-loop` / `data-segment-count` mirror the summary so tests and a future drive-mode HUD can read the topology without parsing the text). `tests/lib/trackPath.test.ts` (10 new cases under `summarizeTrackPath` covering empty city, single straight one-piece open main segment, two stacked straights two-piece open main segment, a 2x2 closed loop of 90deg corners with `mainSegmentClosesLoop: true`, disconnected components giving `mainSegmentLength < totalPieces`, three isolated pieces with `segmentCount = 3`, multi-cell hairpin counted as one piece in the main segment, total piece dedup across multiple two-piece chains and a lone island, no-mutation guard, the intersection-wired-four-straights walker bound, and a CitySchema round-trip). `e2e/editor.spec.ts` (new `/track-path-spec/edit` spec that asserts the readout does not mount on an empty city, mounts and reads `Pieces in main path: 1 of 1 / Main path: open chain` after one straight is placed, grows to `Pieces in main path: 2 of 2` after a connected straight is placed south of the first, totalPieces grows to 3 with segmentCount 2 after a disconnected straight is placed at `(5, 0)`, collapses back to `data-total-pieces='2'` and `data-segment-count='1'` after the disconnected piece is erased, and unmounts when every piece is erased).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1220/1220 pass (1209 prior + 10 new summarizeTrackPath cases plus 1 net pre-existing test counted differently). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` route-specific JS at 7.66 kB (was 6.85 kB; the new readout, the `buildTrackPath` walk, and the `summarizeTrackPath` projection plus their imports add a small overhead). `npx playwright test --project=chromium` passed 26/26 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs + 20 editor specs including the new track-path-spec).
- Assumptions: `totalPieces` counts unique pieces across every segment (deduped by `Set<Piece>` reference identity) because the substrate's deterministic intersection pass-through can re-enter visited pieces from a sibling arm seeded as a new segment; summing `segment.order.length` would over-count an intersection's pass-through piece. The main segment is always `path.segments[0]` per `buildTrackPath`'s contract (segment id `'main'` for the component containing `city.pieces[0]`), so `summarizeTrackPath` reads index 0 directly rather than searching for `id === 'main'`. The readout mounts only when at least one piece is placed because an empty city has nothing to summarize and the existing `editor-piece-count` paragraph already covers the zero-piece case. The data attributes use stringified booleans (`'true'` / `'false'`) for `data-main-segment-closes-loop` because React serializes the JSX boolean prop `false` as the absent attribute, so a test that needs to distinguish open chain from closed loop on a placed city would silently fail without the explicit string. The readout sits in its own paragraph with its own testid (`editor-track-path-readout`) rather than appending to the existing `editor-piece-count` or `editor-connector-match-readout` text so the prior playwright assertions (e.g. `toHaveText('Connectors matched: 2 of 4')`) continue to pass without rewrites; the new paragraph mounts only when at least one piece exists so the empty-city editor surface stays unchanged. The `buildTrackPath` walk is computed at every render rather than memoized because the editor renders at human pace (one click at a time) and the city's piece count is bounded by `MAX_PIECES_PER_CITY = 256`; a future render-perf slice can memoize via `useMemo` on the city reference if a benchmark identifies a cost. The same memoization concern applies to the existing `cityConnectorGlyphs` walk so the two are consistent. Per slice discipline, the multi-segment-per-intersection walker (where one intersection emits a segment per each of its arms), the geometry layer (sampled centerlines, headings, world-space center, spawn / finish line, checkpoints), the drive-mode integrity HUD that reads the same summary, and the save-time integrity check that gates persistence on a closed-loop city all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the substrate-level summary projection plus the editor toolbar readout that REQ-019 / REQ-064 names.
- GDD coverage: REQ-019 stays `partial` (runtime branching via REQ-032 / REQ-065 still pending). REQ-064 stays `partial` and gains `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` and `e2e/editor.spec.ts` in its implementationRefs / testRefs because the substrate gained its first editor consumer. `docs/gdd/06-city-schema.md` build log entry added recording the `TrackPathSummary` interface, the `summarizeTrackPath` helper, the dedup contract, the test surface, and the file list. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` build log entry added recording the editor toolbar readout, the data attributes, the test surface, and the file list.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Multi-Locator Wheel Contact Substrate: REQ-065

- Branch: `feature/req-065-wheel-contact-substrate`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: flipped REQ-065 from not_started to partial by porting VibeRacer's multi-locator candidate evaluation pattern into a new pure module `src/lib/wheelContact.ts`. The substrate REQ-064 already emits one locator per footprint cell of every placed piece into `path.cellToLocators`; this slice ships the candidate-evaluation layer that returns every locator candidate at a wheel's cell, then picks the closest by an injected `distanceToCenterline` resolver. Without this, multi-cell pieces (hairpin, mega sweep, future arc45 / diagonal) silently report on-street status against the wrong centerline mid-piece and the car falls off. New helpers: `wheelCell(wheelX, wheelZ, cellSize)` mirrors the drive-scene `worldToCell` rounding convention (`row = Math.round(z / cellSize)`, `col = Math.round(x / cellSize)`) but takes the cell size as an argument so the substrate stays free of the drive-scene module's import graph (the `src/lib/` boundary is server-only / pure). `WheelContactCandidate` shape carries a `PathLocator` plus the resolved `Piece` and `OrderedPiece` so the caller's distance function reads the piece directly without a second `city.pieces[index]` lookup. `DistanceToCenterlineFn` resolver type so the geometry layer (sampled centerlines per F-003 / F-004) lands in its own slice without churning this module; the resolver returns a non-negative number, with NaN or Infinity reserved for "no contact" (the picker treats any non-finite distance as off-piece and excludes the candidate). `pieceAnchorDistance(ordered, x, z, cellSize)` default fallback that approximates the centerline by the piece anchor's world-space center so callers can wire the multi-locator selection before sampled centerlines land. `wheelContactCandidates(wheelX, wheelZ, path, cityPieces, cellSize)` reads `path.cellToLocators.get(key)` for the multi-locator path; falls back to `path.cellToOrderIdx.get(key)` for cells that predate the multi-locator emission (defensive: the v1 builder always populates `cellToLocators` for every footprint cell, so the fallback surface is reserved for callers that build a `TrackPath` by hand or for a degenerate cell set produced by a future builder change); returns a fresh array on every call so callers cannot mutate cached state; returns the empty array when the cell is off the path. `WheelContactPick` shape `{ candidate, distance }` returned by the picker. `pickClosestWheelContact(candidates, distanceFn, wheelX, wheelZ)` evaluates each candidate's distance and picks the smallest; treats non-finite distances as off-piece and excludes them; breaks ties by candidate insertion order (first wins) so the pick is deterministic across re-evaluations; returns null on empty list or all-non-finite. `wheelTrackContact(wheelX, wheelZ, path, cityPieces, cellSize, distanceFn)` one-shot helper that resolves the candidates and picks the closest in one call. Tests: `tests/lib/wheelContact.test.ts` (29 cases covering `wheelCell` (origin / cardinal step / half-cell boundary rounding / negative coords / custom cell size), `pieceAnchorDistance` (zero at anchor / Euclidean / negative-offset symmetry / custom cell size), `wheelContactCandidates` (empty path / off-path cell / single-cell piece / multi-cell hairpin per-footprint-cell coverage / two-piece overlap surfaces both candidates / mega sweep insertion-order coverage / cellToOrderIdx fallback / defensive missing-segment short-circuit / fresh-array contract), `pickClosestWheelContact` (null on empty / null when all distances non-finite / picks the only finite candidate when others NaN / picks smallest distance / breaks ties by insertion order / threads wheel coords through to distance fn / does not mutate the candidate list), `wheelTrackContact` (null off-path / single-locator pick / two-overlap closer-piece pick / multi-cell hairpin closer-anchor pick / threads through to injected distance function)).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1209/1209 pass (1180 prior + 29 new wheelContact cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 144 kB unchanged (the new module is server-only / lazy and is not pulled into the drive client bundle yet).
- Assumptions: The module takes `cellSize` as an argument rather than importing `CELL_SIZE` from `src/app/[slug]/driveScene.ts` because the `src/lib/` boundary is the server-only / pure half of the codebase; importing the drive-scene module would pull three.js into the substrate's import graph and break the boundary. The drive scene caller (when REQ-032 lands) will pass `CELL_SIZE` explicitly so the substrate stays portable. The default `pieceAnchorDistance` resolver is intentionally coarse: it approximates the centerline by the piece's anchor cell world center without accounting for piece type, rotation, or footprint extent. A multi-cell piece's anchor is one corner of the footprint, so the caller MUST swap this resolver for a per-piece sampled-centerline resolver before shipping a drivable hairpin / mega sweep; the default fallback is for unit-test wiring and for the not-yet-driving-pieces transitional state. The `cellToOrderIdx` fallback is defensive: the v1 builder always populates `cellToLocators` for every footprint cell of every placed piece, but a future builder change or a hand-edited `TrackPath` could leave a cell addressable only by `cellToOrderIdx`; the fallback surfaces that single locator so existing single-locator consumers stay compatible. The picker's tie-breaking rule (first candidate in insertion order wins) is deterministic across re-evaluations, which matters for a future drive-mode integration that re-evaluates per-frame and would jitter otherwise. Returning null on an empty candidate list is the off-path signal; returning null when every distance is non-finite is the "wheel is on a cell that has candidates but the resolver rejected them" signal (e.g. a sampled-centerline resolver that returns Infinity when the wheel is outside the piece's swept area). The module imports `cellKey` from `@/app/[slug]/edit/snapGrid` because the `cellKey` format is shared across the editor and the substrate; importing it here keeps the cell-key format consistent without duplication. The `TrackPath` type is imported from `src/lib/trackPath.ts` (the substrate REQ-064 module), not from any drive-scene module, so the candidate-evaluation layer stays on the pure side. The `cityPieces` parameter is accepted but currently unused in the resolver path because the `OrderedPiece` already carries the piece reference; the parameter is kept on the API surface so a future caller that wants to look up a piece by its index in `city.pieces` (e.g. for the off-street penalty's per-piece-type tuning) does not need to re-derive the piece from the locator. Per slice discipline, the geometry layer (sampled centerlines per piece type for straight, sweeps, hairpin, mega sweep, arc45, diagonal, intersection), the integration into the drive-scene client (off-street penalty per-wheel evaluation, building-cell per-wheel evaluation, sampled-centerline-distance distance function), and the actual per-wheel offset math (4 wheel locators per car) all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the multi-locator candidate evaluation that REQ-065 names.
- GDD coverage: REQ-065 flipped `not_started` to `partial`. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` build log entry added recording the wheelContact module, the helpers, the default fallback resolver, the picker, the test surface, and the file list.
- Followups: none new. F-002 (VibeRacer track-piece watch list), F-003 (sampled path geometry for arc45 / diagonal), F-004 (wheel contact handling for arc45 / diagonal) all stay open and now have the candidate-evaluation substrate ready to consume the per-piece centerline samples once they land.

## 2026-05-04, Cross-Piece Connector Validator: REQ-019, REQ-064

- Branch: `feature/20260504-022310-validate-connections`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: extended the segment-based path substrate with a substrate-level cross-piece connector validator that surfaces every unmatched (open) port across a city. New `UnmatchedPort` interface in `src/lib/trackPath.ts` carries `pieceIndex` (position in `city.pieces`), `cellRow` / `cellCol` (the absolute cell the port lives on after applying the port's footprint offset), and `dir` (the `Dir` 0..7 compass direction). New `validateConnections(city)` helper walks every placed piece's connector ports through `connectorPortsOf`, builds a port index keyed by `cellKey:dir` once, and threads it through the per-port classifier so the cost stays O(N) over the total port count instead of O(N^2) over all piece pairs. A port is matched (omitted from the result) when the neighbor cell along the compass direction exposes a port in the opposite direction sourced from a different piece (the `neighborOwner !== pieceIndex` guard prevents an intersection's own opposing arm from matching itself across the same source cell). Results are walked in placement order with ports in `connectorPortsOf` order so a UI consumer can rely on a stable diff order. The helper returns a fresh array on every call. New tests `tests/lib/trackPath.test.ts` add 14 cases under `validateConnections (REQ-019, REQ-064)`: empty city returns empty array; a single straight reports both ports as unmatched in S then N order; two stacked straights report only the two outer ends as unmatched; a 2x2 closed loop of 90deg corners (right90 + left90 + right90 at rotation 180 + left90 at rotation 180) returns zero unmatched ports because every port has an opposing neighbor across the shared edge; an intersection alone reports four open ports covering all four cardinal directions; an intersection wired to four straights on each cardinal arm reports the four outer ends of the straights as the only unmatched ports (intersection arms all matched); the source-piece guard for an alone intersection so the four arms stay open even though they could pair-up across opposite directions; two adjacent but non-facing pieces (a straight at rotation 0 and a straight at rotation 90 placed below it) report all four ports as unmatched because the ports do not face each other; two diagonals laid corner-to-corner report exactly one matched pair and two open ports (NE of the lower diagonal vs SW of the upper diagonal); placement-order ordering across three isolated straights placed in a non-monotonic anchor order; fresh-array contract; multi-cell hairpin port coverage where the absolute cells reflect the footprint offsets `(-1, 0)` and `(1, 0)`; an arc45 cardinal-corner pair matched against a 180deg-rotated arc45 partner across the shared NE / SW corner with the open ports being S and N; and a CitySchema round-trip.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1180/1180 pass (1166 prior + 14 new validateConnections cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` route-specific JS at 6.85 kB (unchanged; the new helper is server-side / lazy and is not pulled into the editor client bundle).
- Assumptions: The validator returns substrate data (piece index, absolute cell, direction) rather than pixel-space glyphs so it lives in `src/lib/trackPath.ts` rather than `src/app/[slug]/edit/connectorGlyphs.ts`; the editor's pixel-space view (REQ-019 connector glyphs) and the substrate-level validator are separate concerns: the glyph layer paints individual port markers in editor SVG space; the validator returns a city-wide list a future drive-mode warning HUD or a future save-time integrity check can read without dragging the editor SVG state forward. The `validateConnections` name is chosen over `unmatchedPorts` so the helper reads as the canonical city validity check (returning the zero-length array means "every port is connected"); a future caller can layer on `isCityClosedLoop(city)` or `cityHasUnmatchedPorts(city)` predicates that reduce the validator's output. The walked-in-placement-order contract matters because a UI surface that lists unmatched ports (e.g. "5 ports remain open: 1 on Straight #3, 2 on Intersection #5, 1 on Straight #8, 1 on Diagonal #12") needs a stable order so the list does not jitter across edits; sorting by anchor cell would be tempting but it conflicts with the editor's "list of pieces in the order I placed them" mental model. The cardinal-vs-corner classification check that `portsFaceEachOther` enforces is folded in for free because a cardinal port stepping along its cardinal direction lands on a cardinal-adjacent neighbor and a corner port stepping along its corner direction lands on a diagonal-adjacent neighbor; a port that would land on the same cell as the source piece (e.g. the intersection's own opposing arm) is excluded by the `neighborOwner !== pieceIndex` guard. The validator does not gate placement, autosave, save-time persistence, or drive-mode rendering in v1; the editor's existing `cityConnectorGlyphs` already paints matched markers per glyph, and the new validator is the substrate-level twin that future slices will consume. Per slice discipline, the geometry layer (sampled centerlines, headings, world-space center, spawn / finish line, checkpoints), the multi-segment-per-intersection walker, and the integration into the drive scene client all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the substrate-level validator that REQ-019 / REQ-064 name.
- GDD coverage: REQ-019 stays `partial` (runtime branching via REQ-064 / REQ-065 still pending) but its `implementationRefs` and `testRefs` gain `src/lib/trackPath.ts` and `tests/lib/trackPath.test.ts` because the substrate-level validator landed for the first time. REQ-064 stays `partial` (geometry layer plus the multi-segment-per-intersection walker still gate the `done` flip). `docs/gdd/06-city-schema.md` build log entry added recording the `UnmatchedPort` interface, the `validateConnections` helper, the per-port classifier, the source-piece guard, the placement-order contract, the test surface, and the file list.
- Followups: none new. F-002 (VibeRacer track-piece watch list) stays open.

## 2026-05-04, Multi-Segment Track Path Walker: REQ-064

- Branch: `feature/req-064-multi-segment-walker`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: extended `buildTrackPath(city)` in `src/lib/trackPath.ts` so the substrate emits one `PathSegment` per connected component instead of one segment from `pieces[0]` only. Disconnected pieces no longer drop on the floor: each unvisited piece (in placement order) seeds a new segment, with the segment containing `city.pieces[0]` keeping the canonical id `'main'` and subsequent components getting `'segment-1'`, `'segment-2'`, etc. New helper `segmentIdForIndex(index)` (file-local) maps the segment index to its id. New helper `walkComponent(start, pieces, visitedAnchors)` (file-local) extracts the per-component walker out of the multi-component outer loop so the outer loop owns segment seeding and the walker owns intra-segment state; the walker pushes every visited anchor into `visitedAnchors` so the outer loop skips pieces already absorbed into an earlier segment. New helper `findUnvisitedConnectedNeighbor(piece, port, pieces, visitedAnchors)` (file-local) is the start-exit heuristic for sub-segments: a fresh segment prefers a port that connects to any other unvisited piece in the city so the walk goes toward the rest of the component (not back into an already-walked segment); falls back to the last port from `connectorPortsOf` for a single-piece component. The new walker computes `closesLoop` by checking whether `last.exitPort` connects back to `first.piece` (true loop closure across the segment) instead of relying on the seen-cell guard alone; this lands the explicit closed-loop signal that the substrate did not previously surface. The `cellToOrderIdx` map stays scoped to the `'main'` segment only so existing single-segment consumers (the future drive-side spawn / piece-index lookup) keep their backward-compatible `Map<string, number>` contract; pieces in non-main segments are addressed via `cellToLocators`, which now carries entries for every segment's footprint cells. Updated tests `tests/lib/trackPath.test.ts` (renamed `'drops disconnected pieces from the segment'` to `'emits a separate segment per disconnected piece'` and rewrote it to assert the new two-segment topology including the `cellToLocators.get('5,5')` locator entry under `segment-1`; renamed `'drops a disconnected island piece from the segment'` to `'keeps a disconnected island piece out of the main segment'` and rewrote it the same way; added 9 new cases under `buildTrackPath (REQ-064)`: `'emits one segment per disconnected component in placement order'` (two two-piece chains become `main` and `segment-1` in placement order), `'walks three disconnected components into segment-2'` (three islands at `(0,0)`, `(5,5)`, `(-3,-3)` become `main` / `segment-1` / `segment-2`), `'cellToLocators carries locators for every segment, not just main'` (asserts the per-segment locator routing), `'cellToOrderIdx stays scoped to the main segment'` (asserts the backward-compat invariant), `'emits a multi-cell footprint for each isolated segment correctly'` (mega sweep + hairpin both at multi-cell footprint with locator coverage on every footprint cell of each segment), `'returns segments in placement order, not anchor order'` (a `(10, 10)` first-listed piece still gets `'main'`), `'walks the full main chain even when an island piece is listed first'` (the island piece becomes `main` and the connected chain becomes `segment-1` in their listed order), `'every visited piece appears in exactly one segment'` (sanity invariant: with N pieces, the sum of segment lengths equals N), `'disconnected segments do not share locator entries'` (two islands produce locator lists of length 1, no spurious cross-segment entries).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1166/1166 pass (1157 prior + 11 new trackPath cases minus 2 renamed). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 144 kB unchanged (the trackPath module is server-only / lazy and is not pulled into the drive client bundle yet).
- Assumptions: The first segment id stays `'main'` rather than `'segment-0'` to preserve backward compatibility with the prior REQ-064 substrate's tests and the (forthcoming) wheel-contact callers that may have already started keying off the literal `'main'` id; subsequent segments use the `'segment-N'` numeric form because there is no canonical name for "the second connected component in placement order". Segment seeding walks `city.pieces` in placement order rather than re-sorting by anchor coordinates because a city's piece order is the editor's record of "the order in which the author placed pieces", and an author who places a starter piece first expects that piece's component to be the main segment even if the geometric center of the city has shifted to a later-placed island. The `cellToOrderIdx` map stays a `Map<string, number>` (anchor cell -> main-segment idx) rather than promoting to `Map<string, PathLocator>` because the existing single-segment consumers (the drive-side spawn / piece-index lookup that REQ-031 / REQ-065 will read) expect the simple shape, and the per-segment fan-out already lives in `cellToLocators`; promoting `cellToOrderIdx` would force every consumer to re-derive `(segmentId, idx)` from a numeric index. The start-exit heuristic for sub-segments prefers a port that connects to any other unvisited piece (not just the very-next-piece-in-the-array as the original main-segment heuristic did) because a sub-segment's "next piece" is whatever happens to be adjacent in the component, not a fixed-by-array position; the heuristic still falls back to the last port from `connectorPortsOf` so a single-piece component (like an isolated intersection) stays deterministic. The `closesLoop` computation now checks whether `last.exitPort` connects back to `first.piece` rather than relying on the seen-cell guard inside the walker; the seen-cell guard was originally intended to prevent infinite loops on degenerate inputs (a 2-piece pair walked back and forth) but never actually lit up for a real closed loop because the walker always exits via a different port than it entered through. With the new check the substrate emits a true `closesLoop = true` signal when the segment is a closed loop, and `closesLoop = false` for an open chain; this is the signal the future drive-side spawn / lap-counting layer (REQ-033 / REQ-037) will read, but no v1 consumer reads it yet. The walker still emits one segment per connected component, not one segment per intersection arm: a 4-way intersection wired to four straights still produces one segment that pass-throughs the intersection and three open ports on the intersection that are not walked; the multi-segment-per-intersection branching (where one intersection emits a segment per each of its arms) stays deferred to its own slice when the editor surface needs it (REQ-019). Per slice discipline, the cross-piece connector validator (a `validateConnections` helper that reports unmatched ports city-wide), the geometry layer (sampled centerlines, headings, world-space center, spawn / finish line, checkpoints), the multi-segment-per-intersection walker, and the integration into the drive scene client all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the multi-component walker that REQ-064 names.
- GDD coverage: REQ-064 stays `partial` (the geometry layer plus the multi-segment-per-intersection walker still gate the `done` flip). `docs/gdd/06-city-schema.md` build log entry added recording the multi-segment walker, the per-segment locator routing, the start-exit heuristic, the explicit closes-loop signal, and the test surface.
- Followups: none new. F-002 (VibeRacer track-piece watch list) stays open because REQ-064 is named in the watch list; this slice closes another step of the substrate-port adoption.

## 2026-05-04, Track Path Substrate: REQ-064

- Branch: `feature/req-064-track-path-substrate`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: flipped REQ-064 from not_started to partial by porting the segment-based path substrate to a new pure module `src/lib/trackPath.ts`. New helpers (ported from VibeRacer's `track.ts` Phase 0d substrate): `portCell(piece, port)` returns the absolute cell a port lives on by adding the footprint-cell offset to the piece anchor; `neighborAnchorCell(piece, port)` steps one cell beyond the port host along the port's compass direction so a caller can resolve a neighbor by anchor cell or by footprint cell index; `portsConnect(piece, port, neighbor)` walks the neighbor's ports and reports true when one of them sits on the cell-adjacent neighbor cell facing back via `opposite(port.dir)`; `findConnectedNeighbor(piece, port, pieces)` walks the city pieces and returns the first one whose port connects to the given `(piece, port)`. New types `OrderedPiece` (carries `piece` + `entryPort` + `exitPort` + `entryDir` + `exitDir`; geometry intentionally absent and waiting for F-003 / F-004 / REQ-031 / REQ-032), `PathLocator` (`{ segmentId, idx }` pointer back into a segment's `order` array), `PathSegment` (`{ id, order, closesLoop }`), and `TrackPath` (`{ segments, cellToOrderIdx, cellToLocators }`). New builder `buildTrackPath(city)` walks the connected component starting at `city.pieces[0]`: when `pieces.length >= 2` it prefers the start exit port that connects to `pieces[1]` so the walker walks toward the rest of the city, otherwise it falls back to the second port returned by `connectorPortsOf` (matches VibeRacer's `getStartExitPort` fallback). At each step the walker follows the exit through `findConnectedNeighbor`, terminates on dead end (no neighbor) or loop closure (current cell already in the seen set). For two-port pieces (every v1 type except the 4-way `intersection`) the next exit is the only non-entry port; for the 4-way `intersection` v1 picks the port directly opposite the entry so a pass-through walk reads as the natural continuation (non-pass-through branching at intersections is deferred to the multi-segment walker slice). The builder produces a `TrackPath` with one main-id `PathSegment` for non-empty connected components or zero segments for an empty city; `cellToOrderIdx` is keyed by anchor cell only (one entry per ordered piece) while `cellToLocators` is keyed by every footprint cell of every ordered piece (multi-cell pieces like mega sweep and hairpin emit one locator per footprint cell, all pointing at the same `(segmentId, idx)`, so the future drive-side wheel-contact slice REQ-065 can read every footprint cell of a placed piece). An empty city returns an empty `TrackPath` rather than throwing because VibeCity ships an empty city by default per REQ-010 / REQ-015 unlike VibeRacer which throws because a track must have a start. `tests/lib/trackPath.test.ts` (31 cases: portCell anchor / hairpin / negative coords; neighborAnchorCell cardinal step / multi-cell port walk; portsConnect two stacked straights / far-offset rejection / side-by-side rejection / intersection 4-arm coverage / two diagonals corner-to-corner / cardinal-vs-corner cross rejection; findConnectedNeighbor no-match / first-match / source-piece skip / first-of-multiple; buildTrackPath empty-city / single-straight order / single-piece anchor maps to order 0 / single-piece single-cell footprint produces one locator / two-piece chain order / disconnected island drop / hairpin six-locator footprint emission / mega sweep four-locator footprint emission / intersection pass-through via opposite-direction exit / exitDir + entryDir recorded per ordered piece / finite termination on degenerate two-piece config / CitySchema round-trip / three-piece partially-disconnected city returns only the connected component / cellToOrderIdx keyed by anchor cell only with multi-cell pieces having one anchor entry but six footprint locator entries / fresh-path-on-every-call invariant / intersection alone produces a one-piece order list).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1157/1157 pass (1126 prior + 31 new trackPath cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 144 kB unchanged (the new module is server-only / lazy and is not pulled into the drive client bundle yet).
- Assumptions: The geometry layer is deferred from this slice. `OrderedPiece` carries the entry / exit ports and directions but no centerline samples, headings, world-space center, spawn / finish line, or checkpoints. The drive-side concerns (REQ-031 vehicle physics, REQ-032 wheel contact, REQ-065 multi-locator candidate evaluation, F-003 sampled centerlines per piece type, F-004 wheel contact for arc45 / diagonal) all stay in their own slices. The empty-city contract returns an empty `TrackPath` rather than throwing because VibeCity ships an empty city by default per REQ-010 (fresh slug landing) and REQ-015 (kv unset fallback) unlike VibeRacer which throws because a race track must have a start. The intersection pass-through exit is a v1 simplification: the walker treats a 4-way intersection as a continuation when entered from a cardinal arm by exiting via the opposite arm; non-pass-through branching (turning right or left at an intersection) is deferred to the multi-segment walker slice that ships when the editor's intersection runtime surface (REQ-019) lands. The `cellToLocators` map emits one locator per footprint cell of every ordered piece (multi-cell pieces like mega sweep and hairpin produce four / six locator entries respectively, all pointing at the same `(segmentId, idx)`); a future multi-segment walker can layer additional locators on the same cell key when an intersection cell participates in two segments. `cellToOrderIdx` is keyed by anchor cell only because the consumer (a future drive-side spawn or piece-index lookup) reads at the piece anchor rather than at every footprint cell; `cellToLocators` is the per-footprint-cell index. The `buildTrackPath` builder takes `Pick<City, 'pieces'>` rather than the full `City` so the substrate stays free of the building / mood layers; a future slice that adds path metadata under `City` can extend the input shape. The `findConnectedNeighbor` walk is linear in the piece count (O(N) per port lookup, O(N) per piece visited, O(N^2) for the full builder); `MAX_PIECES_PER_CITY = 256` bounds the cost so a worst-case city pays at most ~65k operations per build, which is well below a frame budget. A future cell-keyed neighbor index can speed this up if a benchmark identifies a cost. The substrate ships ahead of REQ-019 (intersection runtime branching), REQ-032 (drive-side wheel contact), and REQ-065 (multi-locator candidate evaluation) which all consume this vocabulary; the geometry layer plus the multi-segment walker land in their own slices. The new module imports from `@/app/[slug]/edit/snapGrid` for `cellKey` and `pieceFootprintCells`; this is a server / pure boundary so the editor module does not pull React or three.js into the substrate's import graph. Per slice discipline, the geometry layer (sampled centerlines, headings, spawn / finish line, checkpoints), the multi-segment walker (one segment per connected component, one segment per intersection arm), the cross-piece connector validator (a `validateClosedLoop` or `validateConnections` helper that reports unmatched ports), and the integration into the drive scene client all stay deferred to their own slices.
- GDD coverage: REQ-064 flipped `not_started` to `partial`. `docs/gdd/06-city-schema.md` build log entry added recording the trackPath module, the connected-graph helpers, the builder, the test surface, and the file list.
- Followups: none new. F-002 (VibeRacer track-piece watch list) stays open because REQ-064 is named in the watch list; this slice closes the substrate-port side of the adoption.

## 2026-05-04, Editor Hairpin Palette: REQ-060

- Branch: `feature/req-060-hairpin-palette`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: flipped REQ-060 from not_started to partial by porting the hairpin piece's canonical 2x3 footprint and palette entry so a builder can record a hairpin placement at the editor surface ahead of the runtime port (multi-locator wheel contact per REQ-065 still gates a drivable hairpin). New `HAIRPIN_FOOTPRINT` constant in `src/app/[slug]/edit/snapGrid.ts` covers the canonical 2x3 block `{(-1, 0), (-1, 1), (0, 0), (0, 1), (1, 0), (1, 1)}` so the anchor cell sits at the middle row of the block's left column and the W-facing connector ports already encoded in `connectorPortsOf` (REQ-063) hang off the top-left and bottom-left footprint cells. `defaultFootprintForPiece` extended with a `case 'hairpin'` that routes through the existing `rotateFootprint` helper so the canonical 2x3 block rotates into a 3x2 at 90deg, mirrors across the anchor at 180deg, and walks back up at 270deg without any new rotation math. `src/app/[slug]/driveScene.ts` mirrors the constant locally as `HAIRPIN_FOOTPRINT` and extends the local `defaultFootprintForPiece` with the same `case 'hairpin'` per the established duplication pattern so the drive scene stays free of the editor-only dependency tree; `pieceFootprintWorldCells` now resolves all six ground quads from the type alone for hairpins placed without an explicit footprint. `src/app/[slug]/edit/editorState.ts` extends `STREET_PALETTE` with a single `hairpin` entry labeled "Hairpin" placed immediately after the `megaSweepLeft` entry so the multi-cell block stays grouped before the single-cell intersection; the `placePiece` and `erasePiece` reducer paths already iterate `pieceFootprintCells` so they pick up the canonical 2x3 footprint with no edits, mirror-matching the REQ-058 mega sweep pattern. `tests/app/snapGrid.test.ts` (new `HAIRPIN_FOOTPRINT` describe block with three cases covering the 2x3 block shape, deduped cell set, and anchor-cell coverage; four new `defaultFootprintForPiece` cases at rotation 0 / 90 / 180 / 270; a `-0` collapse guard on the rotated offsets; two new `pieceFootprintCells` cases on the type-driven default fallback at rotation 0 and rotation 90; one new `occupiedPieceCells` aggregation case asserting all six cells of a placed hairpin land in the occupied set). `tests/app/editorState.test.ts` (refreshed the `STREET_PALETTE` enumeration test to expect the new `hairpin` entry between `megaSweepLeft` and `intersection`; replaced the prior "does not advertise pieces from later slices" guard with an ordering assertion pinning hairpin immediately after `megaSweepLeft` and before `intersection` plus a label assertion; added a `placePiece with REQ-060 hairpin` block covering placement without an explicit footprint, rotation pass-through, overlap rejection on a bottom-row footprint cell collision, overlap rejection on a top-row footprint cell collision, accept-on-clean-grid, second-hairpin overlap rejection, rotation-aware overlap on a 90deg-rotated footprint, atomic erase via a non-anchor footprint cell, and CitySchema validity). `tests/app/driveScene.test.ts` (two new cases under the multi-cell ground-mesh block covering the type-driven default fallback for hairpin at rotation 0 with anchor at `(4, 4)` covering all six expected absolute cells and the rotation-90 variant at anchor `(5, 5)`). `e2e/editor.spec.ts` (new `/hairpin-spec/edit` spec asserting the Hairpin palette button is visible with the right label and not the default selection, places a hairpin at `(1, 1)` and confirms `data-occupied-count` jumps to 6 with all six footprint cells flipping to `data-cell-occupied="true"`, rejects a second hairpin on an overlapping cell, then erases via a non-anchor footprint cell to confirm atomic removal).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1126/1126 pass (1103 prior + 19 new hairpin cases plus 4 modified palette tests). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` route-specific JS at 6.85 kB (was 6.82 kB; the new palette entry adds a tiny overhead from one extra `<button>` in the rendered list). `npx playwright test --project=chromium` passed 25/25 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs + 19 editor specs including the new hairpin spec).
- Assumptions: The canonical 2x3 footprint matches VibeRacer's `HAIRPIN_FOOTPRINT` (PR #81) so a city saved in either project resolves the same six cells; the anchor cell sits at the middle row of the block's left column so the W-facing connector ports at `(dr: -1, dc: 0)` and `(dr: 1, dc: 0)` (already encoded in `connectorPortsOf` for REQ-063) hang off the top-left and bottom-left footprint cells without any port coordinate change. The editor surface ships ahead of the drive-side runtime concerns: a placed hairpin renders six ground quads in the drive scene because `pieceFootprintWorldCells` reads the type-driven default footprint, but the car will fall off the piece mid-corner until REQ-065 (multi-locator wheel contact) lands; this matches the existing pattern where REQ-058 (mega sweep) shipped its editor surface with the same caveat and where REQ-061 / REQ-062 (arc45 / diagonal) landed schema and palette ahead of their runtime concerns per F-003 through F-006. The label "Hairpin" matches the singular VibeRacer label and reads cleanly in the palette button without abbreviation. The palette ordering places hairpin immediately after `megaSweepLeft` and before `intersection` so the multi-cell block stays grouped before the single-cell intersection; an alternate ordering (hairpin at the end of the corner-connector block) was considered but discarded because the hairpin's connector ports are cardinal (W-facing at rotation 0) so it belongs in the cardinal-only block, not the corner-connector block, and the multi-cell block is the right sub-grouping by footprint shape. The `defaultFootprintForPiece` resolver extension routes through the existing `rotateFootprint` helper rather than declaring a per-rotation lookup table because the 90deg clockwise step `(dr, dc) -> (dc, -dr)` produces the right cells for a hairpin (verified by the unit tests at every cardinal rotation); a future hairpinTight or hairpinWide variant would extend the resolver the same way without needing a per-rotation table. The `case 'hairpin'` is duplicated locally in `driveScene.ts` per the established pattern from REQ-058 so the drive scene stays free of the editor-only dependency tree; a future cleanup slice could extract the resolver to a shared module if a third caller appears, but per slice discipline two consumers is not yet enough to justify the abstraction.
