The fov change is the only slider that requires a live three.js poke (`camera.fov = ...; camera.updateProjectionMatrix()`) because height / distance / lookAhead / followSpeed all flow through the chase rig's `CameraRigParams` and the rig reads them each frame; the fov sits on the perspective camera object directly and a frame-time read of `cameraTuningRef.current.fov` would not change anything until the projection matrix is rebuilt. The orbit camera (used on the empty grid) inherits the live fov via the same code path so an empty-grid pause-menu fov change is also visible immediately on resume. The perspective camera's fov is initialized from `cameraTuningRef.current.fov` instead of the static `CAMERA_FOV = 50` constant so a returning player whose tuning differs sees the right framing on the first rendered frame; the unit-test surface still asserts the constant equals 50 because that constant is preserved (the value is just no longer the source of truth for the live camera). The integration effect's cleanup branch nulls `perspectiveCameraRef.current` so a stale slider change after the scene unmounts (e.g. the player navigates to the editor before the panel onChange fires) cannot poke the disposed projection matrix; the Mute toggle pattern from REQ-068 establishes the precedent. The data attribute mirrors expose the live tuning numbers as decimal strings via React's auto-stringify; the tests assert specific values (`'6.4'` / `'14'` / `'0.12'`) so a future change to the default tuning would surface as a test failure asserting the new value. Per slice discipline, REQ-041 (keyboard rebinding) and REQ-042 (touch mode picker) stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the camera-tuning surface. The panel's testid vocabulary follows the established `drive-<feature>` prefix so the existing testid hierarchy stays consistent.
- GDD coverage: REQ-040 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/10-settings.md` Status remains `partial` (REQ-041 / REQ-042 still not started); a build log entry was added recording the cameraSettings module, the panel component, the DriveSceneClient wiring, the slider bounds and step rationale, the unit and e2e test surface, and the file list.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Mega Sweep Palette and Type-Driven Footprint Default: REQ-058

- Branch: `feature/req-058-mega-sweep-palette`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed REQ-058 by extending the editor street palette with the `megaSweepRight` and `megaSweepLeft` long-radius turns and by porting the type-driven default footprint resolver so the four-cell mega sweep footprint flows through every existing reducer (placement overlap, atomic erase, drive scene ground quads, off-street penalty) without recording an explicit `footprint` field on the persisted piece. New exports in `src/app/[slug]/edit/snapGrid.ts`: `MEGA_SWEEP_RIGHT_FOOTPRINT` (the canonical 2x2 block anchored at the bottom-right cell, ported from VibeRacer's `MEGA_SWEEP_RIGHT_FOOTPRINT` in `trackFootprint.ts`), `MEGA_SWEEP_LEFT_FOOTPRINT` (mirror across the column axis, anchored at the bottom-left cell), and `defaultFootprintForPiece(piece)` that resolves a piece's canonical footprint from its type plus rotation (rotates the 2x2 block clockwise via `(dr, dc) -> (dc, -dr)` per 90deg step and collapses `-0` to `0` so two pieces that resolve to the same footprint produce equal cell objects). `pieceFootprintCells` now falls back to `defaultFootprintForPiece(piece)` when the piece has no explicit `footprint` field, so every existing helper (`occupiedPieceCells`, `placePiece` candidate-cell occupancy check, `erasePiece` footprint match, `editorPreview` overlap detection) inherits the multi-cell behavior automatically. `src/app/[slug]/driveScene.ts` mirrors the same constants and the same resolver locally (matches the established duplication pattern that keeps the drive scene module free of the editor dependency tree) and `pieceFootprintWorldCells` now reads the type-driven default so a placed mega sweep renders all four ground quads instead of leaving three visible holes through the placed piece. The off-street penalty (`offStreetPenalty.ts`) already uses `pieceFootprintCells` so it picks up the multi-cell coverage with no edits. `src/app/[slug]/edit/editorState.ts` extends `STREET_PALETTE` with two new entries `megaSweepRight` (label "Mega Sweep Right") and `megaSweepLeft` (label "Mega Sweep Left") inserted between the single-cell sweep pair and the intersection so the single-cell to multi-cell sweep block stays contiguous; `placePiece` and `erasePiece` already iterate `pieceFootprintCells` so they pick up the canonical 2x2 footprint without further edits, which means a candidate placement that would overlap any of the four footprint cells is rejected (REQ-027) and a click on any of the four footprint cells in erase mode removes the whole piece atomically. `tests/app/snapGrid.test.ts` ships 14 new cases covering the constants invariants (mega sweep right covers 2x2 anchored at bottom-right; left mirror; mirror equivalence between the two), the `defaultFootprintForPiece` resolver (single-cell default for every cardinal-only palette type, 2x2 footprint at rotation 0 / 90 / 180 / 270 for both mega sweep variants, 360 cycle invariance, `-0` collapse to `0`, fresh-array contract on each call), and `pieceFootprintCells` plus `occupiedPieceCells` resolving the type-driven default for a mega sweep without an explicit footprint, honoring an explicit footprint over the type-driven default when both are present, and rotating the type-driven default with the piece rotation field. `tests/app/editorState.test.ts` ships 9 new cases under a new `placePiece with REQ-058 megaSweepRight / megaSweepLeft` block: place without recording an explicit footprint for both variants, reject overlap on any of the four footprint cells for both variants, accept on a clean grid, reject a second mega sweep that overlaps the first, rotate-aware overlap rejection, atomic erase via any footprint cell, CitySchema validity. The palette enumeration test was extended to include `megaSweepRight` / `megaSweepLeft` between the single-cell sweep pair and the intersection; the deferred-piece exclusion guard now only excludes `hairpin` (REQ-060). `tests/app/driveScene.test.ts` ships 4 new cases under the `pieceFootprintWorldCells (REQ-045 multi-cell ground meshes)` block covering the type-driven default fallback for both mega sweep variants, rotation-aware fallback, and `cellToWorld` agreement on every type-driven default cell. `e2e/editor.spec.ts` adds a new `/mega-sweep-spec/edit` spec that asserts the palette buttons render with the right labels, places a mega sweep right at `(1, 1)` and confirms `data-occupied-count` jumps to 4 with all four footprint cells flipping to `data-cell-occupied="true"`, places a mega sweep left at `(5, 5)` and confirms the count and footprint cells, rejects a second mega sweep on an overlapping cell, then erases via any footprint cell to confirm atomic removal.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 828/828 pass (796 prior + 14 new snapGrid cases + 9 new editorState cases + 4 new driveScene cases + 5 new editorState ordering / palette enumeration cases that replaced the deferred-piece guard adjustments). `npm run build` produced a green production build; `/[slug]` route bumped from 139 kB to 140 kB (the local mega sweep constants and resolver in `driveScene.ts` add a small overhead) and `/[slug]/edit` route bumped from 4.5 kB to 4.66 kB (the new snapGrid resolver and the two palette entries). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 20/20 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs + 14 editor specs including the new mega sweep spec).
- Assumptions: The mega sweep is plumbed via a type-driven default footprint resolver rather than by recording an explicit `footprint` field on every placed piece because the canonical hash (REQ-013) drops a footprint that equals the single-cell default and a placed-mega-sweep that records its own 4-cell footprint would hash differently from a hand-edited or future-imported mega sweep with the same logical shape and an omitted footprint. The resolver keeps the persisted shape minimal (just `{ type, row, col, rotation }`) so two cities with the same logical pieces hash identically regardless of how the placement reducer recorded them. The resolver lives in both `snapGrid.ts` and `driveScene.ts` (mirrored locally rather than imported across the boundary) per the established pattern that keeps the drive scene module free of the editor dependency tree; `offStreetPenalty.ts` imports from `snapGrid.ts` directly so it picks up the multi-cell behavior without a local copy. The 90deg clockwise rotation step `(dr, dc) -> (dc, -dr)` matches the editor's canonical clockwise rotation direction so the visual footprint after a 90deg rotate-tool press lines up with the visible piece glyph; this is the same step VibeRacer uses in `trackFootprint.ts`'s `rotateFootprintByRotation`. The 2x2 anchor point (bottom-right for `megaSweepRight`, bottom-left for `megaSweepLeft`) is the canonical anchor in VibeRacer's PR #80 footprint constants; matching the upstream anchor keeps the connector pattern (`S -> E` for right, `S -> W` for left) consistent with VibeRacer so a future port of the segment-based path (REQ-064) reads the same anchor. The mega sweep palette entries sit between the single-cell sweep pair and the intersection so the single-cell to multi-cell sweep block stays contiguous and the intersection still anchors the branching block; this preserves the muscle memory of `Straight` as the first entry (the default selection) and keeps the corner-connector block (arc45 then diagonal) at the trailing edge. The hover preview ghost remains single-cell-anchor for now: a hovered mega sweep candidate only renders the anchor-cell ghost rather than all four footprint cells. The reducer-level overlap rejection still works correctly (the click is rejected when any footprint cell is occupied), so the visible feedback is "click did nothing" rather than "ghost predicted the rejection". A future slice can extend `previewKindFor` to accept the candidate piece type and rotation and return a per-cell preview map; this slice intentionally defers that work to keep the diff PR-sized. The palette enumeration test was tightened in this slice to assert the new ordering (mega sweep pair immediately after the single-cell sweep pair, intersection after the mega sweep pair); the prior test that excluded `megaSweepRight` / `megaSweepLeft` from the deferred-piece guard was relaxed to only cover `hairpin` (REQ-060) since that piece still ships in its own slice. The runtime concerns for the mega sweep (sampled centerlines per F-002 / REQ-064, wheel contact multi-locator per REQ-065, pace notes per F-005, difficulty scoring per F-006) stay deferred to the drive scene scaffold; this slice closes only the editor surface and the multi-cell ground-quad rendering. REQ-059 (multi-cell footprint plumbing) is partially closed by the type-driven default resolver but stays `partial` because the broader plumbing (validateClosedLoop, select / move / flip on the footprint union) is VibeRacer-only vocabulary that VibeCity has not introduced; the editor's place / erase / rotate already operate on the footprint union atomically, which is the part of REQ-059 that VibeCity needs.
- GDD coverage: REQ-058 flipped `not_started` to `done`. REQ-059 stays `partial` because the type-driven default resolver closes the multi-cell footprint substrate for placement / erase / rendering but the broader VibeRacer-specific plumbing (validateClosedLoop, select / move / flip helpers) is not in VibeCity's scope yet. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` Piece palette spec text now records the mega sweep entries; build log entry added. `docs/gdd/06-city-schema.md` build log entry added recording the type-driven default footprint resolver.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-03, Controls Persistence to localStorage: REQ-043

- Branch: `feature/req-043-controls-persistence`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed REQ-043 by adding the persistence substrate the future settings pane (REQ-040 camera tuning, REQ-041 keyboard rebinding, REQ-042 touch mode) consumes. New pure module `src/lib/controlsPersistence.ts` ships `CONTROLS_STORAGE_KEY = 'vibecity.controls'` (separate namespace from VibeRacer so a player tuning both projects keeps independent settings per project), `CONTROLS_STORAGE_VERSION = 1` (envelope version guard), `CameraTuningSchema` covering `height` / `distance` / `lookAhead` / `followSpeed` / `fov` (REQ-040 substrate), `DriveActionSchema` enum mirroring the `DriveAction` vocabulary in `driveControls.ts`, `KeyBindingsSchema` as a record from `KeyboardEvent.code` to drive action (REQ-041 substrate), `TouchModeSchema = 'dual-stick' | 'single-stick'` (REQ-042 substrate), `ControlsPayloadSchema` with all sub-fields optional, `ControlsEnvelopeSchema = { version: 1, controls: ControlsPayloadSchema }`, the `Controls` resolved interface, default literals `DEFAULT_CAMERA_TUNING` (mirrors `cameraRig.ts` chase-far constants: height 6.4, distance 14, lookAhead 6, followSpeed 0.12, fov 60), `DEFAULT_KEY_BINDINGS` (mirrors `driveControls.ts` WASD plus arrow keys), `DEFAULT_TOUCH_MODE = 'dual-stick'`, and the helpers `resolveControls(payload)` / `defaultControls()` / `loadControls()` / `saveControls(patch)` / `clearControls()`. `loadControls` falls through to defaults on the server (no `window`), on a missing storage key, on malformed JSON, on a wrong-version envelope, on a payload that fails inner schema validation, and on a localStorage that throws. `saveControls` validates the patch via `ControlsPayloadSchema`, reads any previously-saved payload directly off localStorage so a concurrent-tab write to an unrelated field is preserved on merge, then writes a fresh v1 envelope; returns `false` on the server, on a malformed patch, or on a storage throw. `clearControls` removes the key; returns `false` on the server or a storage throw. `tests/lib/controlsPersistence.test.ts` ships 57 unit tests covering the storage key namespace, the version invariants, every schema accept / reject path, the default values, the merge semantics of `resolveControls`, the malformed-payload fallbacks of `loadControls`, the round-trip / concurrent-tab merge / conflict / empty-patch behavior of `saveControls`, the `clearControls` behavior, and a namespace-separation invariant. New `docs/gdd/10-settings.md` GDD section file with `Status: partial` (REQ-043 done; REQ-040 / REQ-041 / REQ-042 still `not_started` because the UI surfaces ship in their own slices) and a build log entry.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 796/796 pass (739 prior + 57 new controlsPersistence cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with no route-size change (the new module is server-loadable but has no runtime caller in v1 yet; bundle impact lands when REQ-040 / REQ-041 / REQ-042 ship the settings UI).
- Assumptions: The persistence layer ships ahead of the UI surfaces because the storage shape is the contract every future pane reads from and writes to. A future settings pane slice can wire its handlers against `loadControls` / `saveControls` without re-deriving the schema or the defaults. The default literals (`DEFAULT_CAMERA_TUNING`, `DEFAULT_KEY_BINDINGS`, `DEFAULT_TOUCH_MODE`) are duplicated as static literals rather than imported from `cameraRig.ts` / `driveControls.ts` because importing those modules would pull `three.js` into any surface that loads `controlsPersistence.ts` (the home page would bloat by the drive runtime). The duplication is the cheapest isolation point; if a future tuning slice changes a default in the runtime constant, it also updates the matching literal here, and a unit test asserts the exact value. The envelope version is a literal `1` so a future migration that breaks the schema can detect a stale payload by a version mismatch and reset to defaults rather than silently feed a malformed shape into the runtime; v1 does not implement a salvage migration path, the loader just returns defaults. `saveControls` reads the persisted payload directly off localStorage before merging (rather than capturing a snapshot at module load time) so a cross-tab write to an unrelated field is preserved on the next save in this tab. Empty patches are a successful write that records a v1 envelope with empty controls; a future caller that wants to reset to defaults uses `clearControls` instead of `saveControls({})`. The SSR safety check uses `typeof window === 'undefined'` so the helpers are safe to call from a server-rendered surface; this matches the convention already used by Next.js client components that touch browser globals. The schema's `KeyBindingsSchema` allows an empty record because a future settings UI might let a player remove a default binding without setting a replacement; the runtime integrator already ignores unmapped keys so an empty record is a valid (if unusable) state. The schema's `CameraTuningSchema` rejects negative heights / non-finite values / fov above 179 / followSpeed outside `[0, 1]` so a future slider UI cannot persist a value that would crash the camera; the bounds are conservative (followSpeed at 1 produces an instant snap; fov at 179 produces a fisheye but still renders). The `DriveActionSchema` is a closed enum so a future action vocabulary expansion (e.g. `'handbrake'`) requires a coordinated change in `controlsPersistence.ts` and `driveControls.ts`; this is intentional because the persistence layer is the contract the runtime consumes. Per slice discipline, the settings pane UI itself (a sliders / inputs panel that calls these helpers) stays deferred to REQ-040 / REQ-041 / REQ-042; this slice ships only the persistence substrate.
- GDD coverage: REQ-043 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/10-settings.md` created with `Status: partial` and a build log entry; REQ-040 / REQ-041 / REQ-042 status unchanged because the UI surfaces ship in their own slices.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Build / Drive Transition Curtain: REQ-055

- Branch: `feature/req-055-scene-transition-curtain`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed REQ-055 by adding a per-Link transition curtain that paints over the viewport while Next.js completes navigation between the editor (`/<slug>/edit`) and the drive view (`/<slug>`). Pillar 1 ("Build it. Drive it. Build more.") wants the toggle to feel like one click. Both routes already use `<Link prefetch>` so the data-side cost is paid before the click; the missing piece was the perceived feel during the in-flight tick. New pure module `src/app/[slug]/sceneTransition.ts` ships `SCENE_TRANSITION_BACKGROUND = '#101820'` (matches the drive scene clear color so a curtain reads as the next surface arriving rather than a generic loader), `SCENE_TRANSITION_FOREGROUND = '#f7f4ee'` (matches the editor / drive HUD cream so the label reads against the dark backdrop), `SCENE_TRANSITION_FADE_MS = 180` (below the 200 ms perceived-instant threshold so a prefetched navigation reads as a flicker, not a transition), `SCENE_TRANSITION_Z_INDEX = 100` (above the drive scene pause menu's `zIndex: 10` so the curtain owns the surface during navigation), `SCENE_TRANSITION_TESTID_PREFIX = 'scene-transition-curtain'`, the `SceneTransitionTarget = 'drive' | 'edit'` union (the only navigation v1 promises), the `SCENE_TRANSITION_LABEL = { drive: 'Loading drive view', edit: 'Loading editor' }` per-target accessible label table, and pure helpers `sceneTransitionTestid(target)` and `sceneTransitionLabel(target)`. New `'use client'` component `src/app/[slug]/SceneTransitionCurtain.tsx` calls `useLinkStatus()` from `next/link` to read the in-flight pending flag of the nearest enclosing `<Link>`; when pending is true the curtain mounts a fixed full-viewport overlay (`position: 'fixed'`, `inset: 0`, the constants above, `role='status'`, `aria-live='polite'`, `pointerEvents: 'auto'` so an accidental second click while the curtain is up does not re-fire navigation); when pending is false the component returns `null`. `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` imports the curtain and wraps the existing toolbar Drive CTA's `<Link>` children with `<SceneTransitionCurtain target='drive' />`. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` imports the curtain and wraps three `<Link>` instances (top-right Edit CTA, empty-state Open editor CTA, pause-menu Edit CTA) with `<SceneTransitionCurtain target='edit' />`. `tests/app/sceneTransition.test.ts` ships 20 cases covering the constants invariants (background dark enough to read as a curtain via channel-sum check; foreground light enough for contrast; fade ms positive and at most 200; zIndex positive integer above the pause menu zIndex 10; testid prefix kebab-case and matches the documented value), the `SCENE_TRANSITION_LABEL` table (entry per target, mentions the target verbally, no race vocabulary per REQ-037, distinct labels per target), `sceneTransitionTestid` (prefix-target shape, unique per target, kebab-case), and `sceneTransitionLabel` (matches table entry, idempotent). `e2e/editor.spec.ts` adds a "build / drive transition curtain is wired but dormant by default" spec asserting the `scene-transition-curtain-drive` testid resolves to zero elements with no navigation in flight. `e2e/drive.spec.ts` asserts the same dormant contract for the `scene-transition-curtain-edit` testid in the scaffold spec.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 739/739 pass (719 prior + 20 new sceneTransition cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` route-specific JS at 4.5 kB (was 4.22 kB; the curtain wiring and the `useLinkStatus` import account for the small bump). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 19/19 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs with the new curtain dormant assertion + 13 editor specs including the new curtain dormant assertion).
- Assumptions: The curtain uses `useLinkStatus()` from Next.js 15's `next/link` because that hook is the supported way to read a Link's in-flight pending flag without re-implementing the navigation lifecycle in user code; the hook flips true the moment Next.js starts the navigation and back to false when the destination route's first render lands, which is exactly the curtain's lifetime. Mounting the curtain as a descendant of each existing `<Link>` (rather than as a global overlay paired with a custom Link wrapper) is the smallest change that closes the perceived feel: each Link owns its own curtain, the curtain is dormant until that specific Link's click fires, and there is no shared global navigation state to synchronize. The curtain is purely visual: it does not block the click that triggers the navigation (Next's `<Link>` already handles that and the curtain only mounts after the click has been accepted), and it does not call `router.push` or `router.prefetch` itself. The fade duration (180 ms) is intentionally below the 200 ms perceived-instant threshold so a prefetched navigation reads as a flicker rather than a transition; a longer fade would feel sluggish and a shorter fade would not register. The z-index (100) sits above the drive scene's pause menu (zIndex 10) so a curtain that appears while the pause menu is up still owns the surface; without this a slow navigation could leak the pause menu's chrome through the curtain. The `pointerEvents: 'auto'` keeps an accidental second click from re-firing the navigation while the curtain is up; the click target is non-interactive so a stray click on the curtain itself is a no-op. The label copy (`'Loading drive view'` / `'Loading editor'`) is intentionally minimal: a noun phrase the user can map to the click they just made, no spinner verb. The label avoids race vocabulary (`lap`, `race`, `checkpoint`, `finish`, `leaderboard`) so REQ-037's anti-feature lockdown stays in force even during navigation; the unit tests assert this defensively. The constants module duplicates the cream / dark color palette already used by the drive scene and editor rather than importing from `driveScene.ts` because the curtain is a navigation-time concern that should not depend on the scene module's three.js types. The `SceneTransitionTarget` union has only two members (`'drive'` and `'edit'`) because the build / drive toggle is the only navigation pillar 1 promises; future surfaces (a settings pane modal, a version picker) would not navigate between routes so they do not need a curtain. The unit tests do not import the React component because Vitest runs in node and the component depends on `useLinkStatus()` which only resolves inside a Next.js navigation context; the playwright assertions cover the rendered shell. The dormant-contract assertions check that `getByTestId(...)` resolves to zero elements when no navigation is in flight rather than checking for the curtain's display state because `useLinkStatus().pending` is false by default and the curtain returns `null` in that case, so the testid is genuinely absent from the DOM. Per slice discipline, the visible-during-navigation assertion (asserting the curtain appears while a click is in flight) waits for F-008 (seed a non-empty city in playwright so visible-movement assertions land) because the click would only be observably visible against a real navigation latency; the dormant contract is the v1 surface area. A future slice that adds a "reduced motion" toggle in the settings pane (REQ-040 onward) could read from the constants module without touching the curtain or the helper.
- GDD coverage: REQ-055 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/01-vision-and-pillars.md` gained a build log entry recording the curtain wiring; the canonical-spec text already promises pillar 1's "one click" feel so the spec is unchanged.
- Followups: none new. F-008 stays open; once a non-empty city is seeded in playwright, an e2e spec can also assert the curtain mounts visibly during a click-in-flight.

## 2026-05-04, Drive Mode Race-HUD Anti-Feature Lockdown: REQ-037

- Branch: `feature/req-037-anti-feature-lockdown`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed REQ-037 by making the "no laps, no checkpoints, no race HUD" anti-feature machine-checkable so a future slice cannot quietly add race vocabulary to the drive surface without a test failing. New pure module `src/app/[slug]/driveAntiFeatures.ts` ships `RACE_HUD_FORBIDDEN_TERMS` (a lowercase, deduplicated list of forbidden race vocabulary covering `lap`, `laps`, `checkpoint`, `checkpoints`, `lap timer`, `lap counter`, `finish line`, `race time`, `best time`, `fastest lap`, `leaderboard`, `standings`, `podium`), `RACE_HUD_FORBIDDEN_TESTIDS` (a list of forbidden testids prefixed `drive-` covering `drive-lap-timer`, `drive-lap-counter`, `drive-checkpoint`, `drive-checkpoint-banner`, `drive-race-timer`, `drive-finish-line`, `drive-leaderboard`, `drive-standings`), and `containsRaceHudVocabulary(text)` that does a case-insensitive whole-word match (single-word terms split the input on alphanumeric boundaries via `[a-z0-9]+` so substrings like `elapsed` / `flap` / `clap` / `overlapping` / `checkpointing` do not trigger a false positive; multi-word terms match contiguous lowercase substrings; non-string inputs and the empty string return false defensively). `tests/app/driveAntiFeatures.test.ts` ships 18 cases covering the constants invariants (non-empty list, lowercase / trimmed / unique terms, drive-prefixed unique testids, headline race vocabulary present), the matcher (bare forbidden single-word terms, case variations of the same, multi-word terms, sentence context, substring-safe handling for the false-positive set above, HUD vocabulary VibeCity actually ships stays clean across `throttle` / `brake` / `steer left` / `Speed wu/s` / `M: mute engine` / `Esc: pause` / `R: respawn`, empty string and non-string inputs return false), and a defensive sweep that asserts every line of `HUD_CONTROLS_HINT_LINES` plus `HUD_SPEED_LABEL` and `HUD_SPEED_UNIT` are free of race vocabulary so a copy edit cannot smuggle race terms into the live HUD. `e2e/drive.spec.ts` imports `RACE_HUD_FORBIDDEN_TESTIDS` and asserts each resolves to zero elements on both the scaffold spec and the empty-state spec so a future regression cannot ship a race-HUD panel without the assertion failing, regardless of whether the car-mounted gate has fired. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` gained a Race-HUD anti-feature lockdown section documenting the constants and the test surface, the Out-of-scope entry for REQ-037 was reworded to point at the active enforcement, and a build log entry was added.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 719/719 pass (701 prior + 18 new driveAntiFeatures cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS unchanged at 139 kB (the new module is small and is only imported by the test surface and the e2e suite, not the runtime drive scene). The e2e suite was not run locally in this slice; the playwright assertions are additive and the unit tests cover the constants the e2e suite reads from.
- Assumptions: The forbidden-term list is conservative (the headline race vocabulary plus a few near-synonyms like `podium` and `standings` for completeness) rather than exhaustive because a longer list would risk false positives on innocuous future copy. A future slice that needs a term that overlaps innocuously can scope the vocabulary check to a smaller surface (the unit tests already scope to the specific HUD strings rather than the whole source tree). The matcher uses a whole-word match for single-word terms because `lap` is the easiest forbidden term to false-positive on (`elapsed`, `flap`, `clap`, `overlapping` all contain it as a substring) and the existing drive-mode docblock comments already use `lap` in phrases like "the elapsed pause duration" so a substring-only matcher would trip on the source itself; multi-word terms (`lap timer`, `finish line`) are matched as contiguous substrings because the inverse risk does not exist. The forbidden-testid list is not exhaustive but covers the headline race-HUD elements a future slice would most plausibly ship; if a future slice introduces a new testid pattern (e.g. a settings pane testid), this list does not need to grow because the lockdown's purpose is to prevent racing UI, not to whitelist non-racing UI. The e2e assertion runs on both the scaffold and empty-state specs because a regression could plausibly ship a race surface that renders before the car-mounted gate fires; checking only the populated case would miss that class of bug. The matcher returns false for non-string inputs (numbers, null, undefined, objects, arrays) because the helper is also called from defensive sweeps where a tuning bug could leak a non-string into a HUD overlay; throwing in that case would mask the bug behind a test failure that points at the matcher rather than at the leak. The new module is intentionally not imported by `DriveSceneClient.tsx` because the runtime scene does not need to consult the lockdown; the lockdown is a test-time contract. Per slice discipline, REQ-040 onward (settings pane), REQ-035 (touch input), and any future race-mode opt-in (which the vision explicitly does not promise) all stay deferred to their own slices.
- GDD coverage: REQ-037 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` Race-HUD anti-feature lockdown section added with a build log entry; the Out-of-scope list entry for REQ-037 was reworded from "explicit" to "actively enforced via the Race-HUD lockdown section above".
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Drive Mode Minimap: REQ-069

- Branch: `feature/req-069-drive-minimap`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed REQ-069 by adding a top-right minimap so a player can read the city silhouette and the live car position at a glance without leaving the drive surface. New pure module `src/app/[slug]/driveMinimap.ts` ships `MINIMAP_SIZE_PX = 160` (square viewport so the SVG coordinate scale is uniform on both axes), `MINIMAP_PADDING_CELLS = 1` (one-cell halo around the city bounding box so an edge piece does not render at the literal viewport edge), `MINIMAP_PIECE_COLOR = '#7d6b4a'` matching the editor piece brown, `MINIMAP_BUILDING_COLOR = '#6b7d4a'` matching the editor building olive, `MINIMAP_BACKGROUND_COLOR` / `MINIMAP_BORDER_COLOR` for the panel chrome, `MINIMAP_CAR_COLOR = '#c0392b'` matching `CAR_BODY_COLOR`, `MINIMAP_CAR_SIZE_PX = 10`, the `MinimapBounds` type carrying `{ minX, maxX, minZ, maxZ, width, depth, scale, offsetX, offsetY, sizePx }`, `minimapBoundsForCity(pieces, buildings, paddingCells?, sizePx?)` that letterboxes the city footprint into the square viewport (longer axis fills, shorter axis centers via offset; reuses `pieceFootprintWorldCells` from `driveScene` so multi-cell pieces expand correctly; returns `null` on empty city or non-positive `sizePx` so the caller can skip rendering), `worldToMinimap(x, z, bounds)` that projects world coords to SVG viewport pixels (north stays at the top because world `+z` is south and SVG `+y` is down, so a smaller `z` renders at a smaller `y`; non-finite inputs collapse to NaN coords so the SVG layer never paints garbage), and `headingToMinimapDegrees(headingRadians)` that converts the integrator heading to CSS rotation degrees (0 = north, +90 = east, matching the SVG clockwise rotation convention; non-finite collapses to 0). `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` adds a `minimapCarRef` SVG group ref, memoizes `minimapBounds` via `useMemo` keyed on `city.pieces` / `city.buildings`, ships an `updateMinimap` helper inside the integration effect that writes the live `translate(...) rotate(...)` transform onto the SVG car group each tick (so the React tree does not re-render every frame) and mirrors `data-minimap-car-x` / `data-minimap-car-y` / `data-minimap-car-rotation` on the scene root; the helper is called from the initial post-mount sync, the per-frame integration tick (after `updateHud`), and the respawn handler (after `updateHud`) so every state change that moves the car also moves the marker. `minimapBounds` is added to the integration effect's dep array so the helper closure captures the live bounds. The JSX renders a fixed-position panel (top: 64, right: 16, below the Edit / Sound buttons) sized to `MINIMAP_SIZE_PX` only when `hasVehicle && !showPauseMenu && minimapBounds` containing an SVG with one `<rect>` per piece footprint cell (sized to `CELL_SIZE * scale` and centered via the `worldToMinimap` projection), one `<rect>` per building (sized to 0.85 of the cell to differentiate from pieces), and one rotated `<polygon>` for the car. The panel carries `pointerEvents: 'none'` so it never steals clicks. `data-minimap-visible` / `data-minimap-piece-count` / `data-minimap-building-count` attributes ride on the scene root so a test can read the panel state without inspecting the SVG. `tests/app/driveMinimap.test.ts` ships 30 cases covering the constants invariants (positive size, color hex shapes, distinct color vocabulary across piece / building / car, car size below viewport / 4), `minimapBoundsForCity` (empty city returns null, non-positive size override returns null, single piece scale correct, default padding inflates bounds, explicit padding override widens, wide city letterboxes with `offsetY > 0`, tall city letterboxes with `offsetX > 0`, building-only bounds, piece-and-building mixed bounds, negative-coords symmetry with positive-coords, multi-cell footprint expansion), `worldToMinimap` (city min corner projects to viewport origin offset, max corner to far edge along the longer axis, center to viewport center, north at top via z mapping, NaN passthrough for non-finite inputs, linear distance scaling), and `headingToMinimapDegrees` (0 / 90 / 180 / 270 cardinal cases, negative-radian symmetry, non-finite collapses to 0, finite output for any finite input). `e2e/drive.spec.ts` asserts `data-minimap-visible='false'` / `data-minimap-piece-count='0'` / `data-minimap-building-count='0'` on the scaffold spec and confirms the `drive-minimap` testid resolves to zero elements; the empty-state spec confirms the same hidden-by-default contract since the playwright webServer runs without KV so `loadCity` returns the empty city and the bounds come back null.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 701/701 pass (671 prior + 30 new driveMinimap cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 139 kB (was 138 kB; the minimap module and SVG overlay add a small overhead, no new dependency). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 18/18 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs with the new minimap assertions + 12 editor specs).
- Assumptions: The minimap is an SVG overlay rather than a three.js scene render-to-texture because the city silhouette is a static 2D layout that does not benefit from the 3D scene's lighting / depth / camera and an SVG layer is cheaper to update imperatively each tick. Letterboxing the city bounds into a square viewport (longer axis fills, shorter axis centers) keeps the projection scale uniform on both axes; a stretched fit would distort the city's aspect ratio and lie about angular relationships. The viewport is 160 px square because that is large enough to read at glance distance from the bottom-right HUD without dominating the drive surface; smaller would lose silhouette detail and larger would cover too much of the canvas. The panel sits at top: 64, right: 16 because the Edit / Sound toggles already occupy top: 16; placing the minimap below them keeps every overlay visible without overlapping. The piece color (piece brown) and building color (building olive) match the editor's vocabulary so a player who built the city in the editor recognizes the same color language on the minimap. The car color matches `CAR_BODY_COLOR` so the player vehicle reads as the same red dot in both views. The car is rendered as a heading-rotated triangle (apex at the front, two trailing corners at the rear) so a player can disambiguate forward direction at a glance; an isotropic dot would lose heading information. The triangle's apex points up at heading 0 because heading 0 is north and SVG rotation 0 keeps the triangle in its declared orientation; SVG rotates clockwise so heading +PI/2 (east) rotates the triangle 90 degrees clockwise to point right, which matches the world projection where +x is east. The minimap panel renders only when `hasVehicle && !showPauseMenu && minimapBounds` so the empty grid keeps it dormant (no city footprint to project) and the pause menu owns the click surface (REQ-039); the bounds are also null on an empty grid so the helper short-circuits before the JSX would render anything. The integration loop writes the live transform imperatively via a ref so the React tree does not re-render every frame; this matches the `data-car-*` and `data-hud-speed` mirror patterns already used by REQ-034 and REQ-066. The data attributes on the scene root expose the projection state so a future visible-movement test (F-008) can assert the marker tracks the car without inspecting the SVG transform string. The piece silhouette uses one rect per footprint cell (via `pieceFootprintWorldCells`) so multi-cell pieces (mega sweep, hairpin, future arc45 / diagonal once REQ-059 lands) show their full footprint; v1's palette ships single-cell pieces so each piece contributes one rect today, and the helper is correct for any future palette additions. The building rect is 0.85 of the cell size (mirroring the drive scene's building extrusion which is also 0.85 of `CELL_SIZE`) so the building reads as visually distinct from a same-cell piece without forcing a separate color check. The minimap helper module imports `pieceFootprintWorldCells` from `driveScene` (single source of truth for the footprint expansion) rather than re-implementing it; this matches the pattern established by `offStreetPenalty`'s reuse of the editor's `pieceFootprintCells`. Per slice discipline, REQ-040 onward (settings pane that lets the minimap size / padding / colors be tuned), REQ-035 (touch input with a tap-to-zoom on the minimap), and a click-to-teleport interaction all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the read-only overlay.
- GDD coverage: REQ-069 added to `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` as a new row (status `done`). `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` Minimap section added with a build log entry; the canonical-spec file header lists REQ-069 alongside the rest of the drive-mode requirements.
- Followups: none new. F-008 (seed a non-empty city in playwright so visible-movement assertions land) stays open and now has the minimap car marker ready to verify alongside the chase camera, the car movement, the building / off-street penalties, the HUD readout, and the engine audio mute once the seed mechanism lands.

## 2026-05-04, Drive Engine Audio: REQ-068

- Branch: `feature/20260503-225722-engine-audio`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed the drive-mode silence gap. The car now emits a Web Audio engine note whose pitch and volume track the live speed. New pure module `src/app/[slug]/engineAudio.ts` ships tuning constants (`ENGINE_IDLE_FREQUENCY_HZ = 80`, `ENGINE_MAX_FREQUENCY_HZ = 360`, `ENGINE_IDLE_GAIN = 0.04`, `ENGINE_MAX_GAIN = 0.18`, `ENGINE_FILTER_CUTOFF_HZ = 1200`, `ENGINE_SMOOTHING_SECONDS = 0.08`, `ENGINE_MUTE_KEY_CODE = 'KeyM'`), pure helpers `engineFrequencyForSpeed(speed, maxSpeed?)` and `engineGainForSpeed(speed, maxSpeed?)` (linear interpolation between idle and peak with `|speed|` so reverse climbs the same way as forward, NaN / Infinity collapse to idle, custom maxSpeed override for REQ-040 future settings), an `EngineAudioContextLike` minimal interface so the unit tests can pass a fake context, and the `EngineAudioRig` class that owns a sawtooth oscillator routed through a lowpass biquad filter into a gain node clamped well below clipping (peak 0.18 so other future audio sources can layer on top) with a `start()` / `update(speed)` / `setMuted(muted)` / `stop()` lifecycle. The rig's `start()` resumes a suspended `AudioContext` (browser autoplay policy) and ramps the gain to the idle level via `setTargetAtTime` so the resume does not click; `update` is a no-op before start or while muted so the integration loop can call it unconditionally; `setMuted(true)` ramps the gain to zero without tearing down the rig so unmute is instant; `stop()` ramps to zero, stops the oscillator (with a Safari double-stop throw guard), and disconnects the chain. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` adds an `engineMuted` React state (with `engineMutedRef` synced via `useEffect` so the keydown handler reads the live value), an `engineRigRef` carrying the live rig handle, a `handleToggleEngineMute` `useCallback` that flips the state and also calls `rig.setMuted` if the rig exists, and a new `ensureEngineAudio` helper inside the integration effect that lazily builds the rig on the first user gesture using `window.AudioContext ?? webkitAudioContext` (try / catch swallowed so a locked-down browser keeps the drive scene playable without sound). The existing `handleKeyDown` body calls `ensureEngineAudio` after the bound-key check so the first throttle / brake / steer keypress is the gesture; a new `handleEngineMuteKey` window keydown listener gated on the car-mounted branch flips the mute toggle (M key) and also counts as the first user gesture. The per-frame integration tick calls `rig.update(vehicle.speed)` after the HUD update; the pause branch calls `rig.update(0)` so the engine falls back to idle while paused so the menu reads against quiet; the respawn handler calls `rig.update(0)` so a held-throttle wreck does not carry over engine note on respawn. The cleanup branch removes the new keydown listener and calls `rig.stop()` so navigating away does not leak an oscillator. The JSX renders a `drive-engine-mute-toggle` button at the top right (left of the existing Edit CTA) only when `hasVehicle`, with `aria-pressed` mirroring the live mute state and a Sound on / Sound off label so a click toggles silence without leaving the drive surface; the controls hint gains an `M: mute engine` line so a first-time visitor sees the binding alongside the rest of the vocabulary. `data-engine-audio-muted` and `data-engine-audio-started` attributes ride on the scene root so a test can read the rig state without inspecting the AudioContext. `tests/app/engineAudio.test.ts` ships 46 cases covering the constants invariants (idle below peak, peak below clipping, filter cutoff above peak pitch, smoothing positive and short), `ENGINE_MUTE_KEY_CODE === 'KeyM'` plus collision guards against `DEFAULT_KEY_BINDINGS` / `PAUSE_KEY_CODE` / `RESPAWN_KEY_CODE`, the frequency / gain helpers (idle at rest, peak at MAX_SPEED, clamp above MAX_SPEED, magnitude for reverse, linear interpolation, NaN / Infinity collapse to idle, custom maxSpeed override), `EngineAudioRig` construction (sawtooth oscillator at idle freq, lowpass filter at cutoff, gain starts at zero, chain wired oscillator -> filter -> gain -> destination, oscillator does not start at construction), `start()` (oscillator started, gain ramped to idle, suspended-context resume, no resume on running context, idempotent), `update()` (frequency and gain track speed, clamp at MAX_SPEED, magnitude for reverse, no-op before start, no-op while muted), `setMuted()` (ramps to zero on mute, ramps back to idle on unmute, no oscillator start on pre-start unmute), `stop()` (oscillator stopped and disconnected, gain ramped to zero, idempotent, no-op before start, double-stop throw swallowed). The fake context is built from `vi.fn` mocks so the tests run without spinning up Web Audio. `e2e/drive.spec.ts` asserts `data-engine-audio-muted='false'` / `data-engine-audio-started='false'` on both the scaffold and empty-state specs and confirms the `drive-engine-mute-toggle` testid resolves to zero elements (the playwright webServer runs without KV so the city is empty and the listener gate prevents the rig from being built without a car); pressing M on the empty grid is a no-op since the listener gate prevents the rig from being built without a car.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 671/671 pass (625 prior + 46 new engineAudio cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build (one prior eslint warning fixed by adding `handleToggleEngineMute` to the integration effect's dep array; `useCallback` with empty deps so the dep is stable). `npx playwright test e2e/drive.spec.ts` reported 2/2 pass.
- Assumptions: The rig is built lazily on the first user gesture rather than on mount because every modern browser gates `AudioContext` creation on a user gesture (Chrome's autoplay policy, Safari's silent autoplay block); a mount-time construction would log an autoplay warning and the resume path would not work without a click anyway. The rig is left running across pause toggles (the integration loop just calls `update(0)` while paused) instead of stopping and restarting because tearing down and rebuilding the AudioContext costs hundreds of milliseconds and the smoothing ramp would re-click on every resume. The mute toggle is exposed as both an M keyboard binding and a top-right button so a player using touch (REQ-035 deferred) still has a visible control without keyboard access; the M key was picked because it does not collide with `DEFAULT_KEY_BINDINGS` / `PAUSE_KEY_CODE` / `RESPAWN_KEY_CODE` and is the conventional arcade-driving mute key. The peak gain (0.18) is clamped well below clipping so future UI clicks (next slice candidate) and collision sounds can layer on top without saturating the master. The high-cut filter cutoff (1200 Hz) is above the peak oscillator pitch (360 Hz) so the sawtooth keeps body but reads as engine rumble instead of a buzzsaw; harmonics above 1200 Hz get attenuated. The smoothing time constant (0.08 s) is short enough to track the integrator's per-frame updates (60 fps = 16.7 ms / frame) but long enough to absorb a sudden speed change without clicking. The `engineMutedRef` mirror keeps the keydown handler's view of the mute state in sync without forcing the integration effect to re-attach listeners on every state change; this matches the `pauseStateRef` pattern already used by REQ-039. The build's prior eslint warning (`useEffect missing dependency`) was resolved by adding `handleToggleEngineMute` to the dep array; the callback is `useCallback` with a stable identity so the effect does not re-run unless the city changes. The rig is omitted on the empty grid because the keydown listener gate (already used by REQ-034 / REQ-039 / REQ-067) keeps the rig dormant on a city with no pieces; this matches the established convention that the empty grid keeps every drive-mode runtime concern (physics, HUD, pause, respawn, audio) dormant. The rig's `update(0)` call on pause / respawn ramps both pitch and gain to idle rather than zero so a paused world still has a faint engine hum; this matches arcade convention (pause does not silence the engine; mute does). The Web Audio API is in the AGENTS.md stack list (Rule 3) so this slice does not introduce a new core dep.
- GDD coverage: REQ-068 added as a new row with status `done`. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` gained an Engine audio section and a build log entry; the canonical-spec list of in-scope drive-mode requirements grew from REQ-031..REQ-067 to include REQ-068.
- Followups: none new. The slice does not depend on F-008 (seed a non-empty city in playwright) but the e2e assertion would benefit from it once that followup lands so a test can press W and observe `data-engine-audio-started='true'` flip on the first throttle press.

## 2026-05-04, Drive Scene Renders One Ground Quad Per Footprint Cell: REQ-045 (multi-cell)

- Branch: `feature/20260503-224914-multi-cell-piece-meshes`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed a latent rendering gap in the drive scene where any street piece carrying an explicit `footprint` (the multi-cell pieces queued under REQ-058 mega sweep, REQ-060 hairpin, and any future arc45 / diagonal once REQ-059 lands) was rendered with a single ground quad at the anchor cell, leaving visual holes over the rest of the footprint while the off-street penalty (REQ-054) correctly treated the full footprint as on-street. The fix is purely additive on the drive side: `src/app/[slug]/driveScene.ts` now exports a pure `pieceFootprintWorldCells(piece)` helper that resolves a piece's `footprint` (or the implicit `[{ dr: 0, dc: 0 }]` default for single-cell pieces) into world-space cell centers, returning `{ row, col, x, z }[]` so the scene mount can drop one quad per cell. The helper duplicates the `DEFAULT_PIECE_FOOTPRINT` constant rather than importing the editor's `pieceFootprintCells` so the drive-scene module stays free of the editor dependency tree (matches the same call-site pattern offStreetPenalty already follows). `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` rewrites the street-piece render loop to iterate `pieceFootprintWorldCells(piece)` and instantiate one `THREE.Mesh` per cell, reusing the shared `PlaneGeometry` (allocated once outside the loop) and the per-piece `MeshLambertMaterial` so the per-cell allocation cost is one Mesh per cell instead of one per piece. The persisted rotation flows through to every sub-quad so the next slice that introduces per-cell glyphs (centerline arrows, lane stripes per F-003) inherits the correct orientation. Single-cell pieces remain a one-quad render because `pieceFootprintWorldCells` returns a single entry, so the change is invariant for every piece in the v1 palette today (mega sweep / hairpin palette entries stay deferred under REQ-058 / REQ-060). `tests/app/driveScene.test.ts` ships 7 new cases under a new `pieceFootprintWorldCells (REQ-045 multi-cell ground meshes)` block: single-cell return shape (one entry at the anchor), `cellToWorld` agreement on the single-cell coords, four-cell mega-sweep-style expansion with offset coverage and per-cell `cellToWorld` agreement, negative-coords plus negative-offset hairpin-style expansion, fresh-array contract on each call, declared-order preservation, and bounds agreement with `cityWorldBounds` (every footprint cell's quad sits inside the city's bounding box).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 625/625 pass (618 prior + 7 new pieceFootprintWorldCells cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS unchanged at 138 kB (the helper is small and the per-frame rendering cost is unchanged because v1 has no multi-cell pieces in the palette today).
- Assumptions: The helper lives in `driveScene.ts` because the drive scene owns the world-space coordinate convention (`CELL_SIZE`, `cellToWorld`); putting the helper in `edit/snapGrid.ts` would force the drive scene to depend on the editor module, violating the slice's "render layer is independent of the editor surface" boundary that already applies to `streetCellSet` (which lives in `offStreetPenalty.ts` for the same reason). Duplicating the `[{ dr: 0, dc: 0 }]` default is intentional: the editor and the drive scene have independent reasons to handle a single-cell piece (the editor for occupancy checks, the drive scene for ground meshes), and importing across the boundary would couple the two modules just to avoid four characters of duplication. The render loop allocates one `MeshLambertMaterial` per piece and shares it across all of that piece's sub-quads; this matches the prior single-quad code's allocation model so the per-piece material count is unchanged and the per-cell cost is just the Mesh wrapper. The dispose sweep in the cleanup branch (`scene.traverse((obj) => { if (obj instanceof THREE.Mesh) { obj.geometry.dispose(); ... } })`) was not changed: it already disposes every mesh's material, so a multi-cell piece with three sub-quads (sharing one material) will dispose the same material three times. `THREE.Material.dispose()` is idempotent so the repeat dispose is a no-op; this is the simplest contract that does not require a separate material-tracking pass and matches the prior building-loop's per-mesh material dispose. The persisted rotation flows through to each sub-quad. For the v1 placeholder color (uniform asphalt grey across cells) this is invisible because a rotated solid-color square reads identically to the unrotated one. When the sampled-centerline visuals (F-003) land each cell will need its own per-cell rotation derived from the sampled centerline at that cell, not the piece's anchor rotation; this slice intentionally does not anticipate that change because three similar lines is better than a premature abstraction. The new helper is named `pieceFootprintWorldCells` to mirror the editor's `pieceFootprintCells` naming so a reader who knows one knows the other; the difference is the world-space `(x, z)` pairing the drive scene needs and the editor does not. Per slice discipline, REQ-058 (mega sweep palette entry), REQ-060 (hairpin palette entry), and REQ-059 (multi-cell footprint plumbing in editor reducers) all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice closes only the drive-side rendering gap so a hand-edited or future-saved multi-cell piece renders correctly today. The helper is also not wired into the placeholder car render path because the car is a single-cell mesh group at the spawn anchor (REQ-047) and its position is driven by the per-frame integration loop, not by piece footprint expansion.
- GDD coverage: no row status flips (REQ-045 is already `done` and the multi-cell coverage was a latent rendering gap not previously surfaced as its own row). `docs/gdd/11-scene.md` gained a build log entry recording the multi-cell ground-mesh rendering contract; the canonical-spec text already requires per-piece quads on the grid so the spec is unchanged.
- Followups: none new. F-002 (track upstream VibeRacer track-piece additions) stays open; when REQ-058 / REQ-060 land their palette entries, the rendering side is already correct so those slices ship only the editor surface plus the schema-level footprint.

## 2026-05-04, City Versions Sorted Set with Newest-N Retention: REQ-052

- Branch: `feature/req-052-versions-history`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed REQ-052 by bounding the per-slug version history and adding the read helper a future "version picker" UI will consume. `src/lib/recentVersions.ts` ships `MAX_CITY_VERSIONS = 50` (per-slug history bound), `DEFAULT_RECENT_VERSIONS_LIMIT = 12` (mirrors the home-page `recentSlugs` default so a viewport-sized list is the cheap default), `MAX_RECENT_VERSIONS_LIMIT === MAX_CITY_VERSIONS` so a reader cannot ask for more than the writer keeps, the `CityVersionEntry = { hash: CityVersionHash, updatedAt: number }` shape, and `recentVersions(slug, limit)` that reads `city:${slug}:versions` newest-first via `kv.zrange(..., { rev: true, withScores: true })` and filters each member through `parseCityVersionHash` so a malformed entry written by an older writer cannot leak into a future deep link UI as a broken pin; KV-unconfigured returns `[]` (mirrors the `recentSlugs` soft-fallback), non-positive limit returns `[]`, non-finite scores skip with a warn. `src/app/api/city/[slug]/route.ts` PUT handler now calls `kv.zremrangebyrank(kvKeys.cityVersions(slug), 0, -(MAX_CITY_VERSIONS + 1))` immediately after the `zadd` so the per-slug history is bounded; the trim is rank-based and a no-op when the set is at or below the cap; the docblock writes-list grew from 5 steps to 6 (trim inserted between zadd-versions and zadd-index). The version payload at `city:${slug}:version:${hash}` is intentionally NOT deleted so a stale `?v=<hash>` deep link to a trimmed-from-history snapshot still loads via the existing `loadCity` missing-version branch (REQ-048, REQ-049). `tests/_fakeKv.ts` gained `zcard` and a Redis-faithful `zremrangebyrank` (negative-index resolution that does not bump out-of-range stop indices into valid range, so e.g. `ZREMRANGEBYRANK 0 -51` on a 2-element set is a no-op as Redis does it; the prior naive `Math.max(0, len + idx)` would have removed the first entry). `tests/lib/recentVersions.test.ts` ships 12 cases covering KV-unconfigured returns `[]`, empty-set returns `[]`, newest-first ordering with timestamps, explicit limit smaller than count, limit greater than count returns the full set, non-positive limit returns `[]`, `MAX_RECENT_VERSIONS_LIMIT` cap (vi.spyOn against `fake.zrange` to confirm stop arg = `MAX_RECENT_VERSIONS_LIMIT - 1`), invalid-member skip with warn, the `cityVersions` namespace key invariant, `MAX_CITY_VERSIONS` positive integer, `MAX_RECENT_VERSIONS_LIMIT === MAX_CITY_VERSIONS`, and `DEFAULT_RECENT_VERSIONS_LIMIT` positive and at most the cap. `tests/app/cityRoute.test.ts` gained 3 PUT cases: the history zset grows on every save (zcard = 2 after two PUTs, newest-first order verified), a saturated-history PUT trims one oldest entry (zcard stays at `MAX_CITY_VERSIONS`, the score-1 synthetic entry is gone via `zscore` returning null, the score-2 entry survives with score 2), and the version payload survives the trim so deep links to trimmed entries still resolve. `docs/gdd/03-persistence.md` flipped `Status: partial` to `Status: done`, updated the key namespace table to record the trim policy, expanded the Write path step list from 8 to 9 (trim inserted between zadd-versions and zadd-index), added a Version history reads section that records the `recentVersions` contract (defaults, KV-unconfigured fallback, malformed-member skip), and added a build log entry.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 618/618 pass (603 prior + 12 new recentVersions cases + 3 new cityRoute REQ-052 cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with no route-size change (the new module is server-only and the trim is one extra KV call). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 18/18 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive + 12 editor specs; the e2e suite covers the success path of the API route via the existing autosave PUT in editor.spec.ts and is unchanged because the trim is a no-op in the under-cap regime). `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` validated.
- Assumptions: `MAX_CITY_VERSIONS = 50` is small enough to bound KV storage under sustained editor churn (autosave debounce is 600 ms per REQ-025, so a 30-second sustained edit streak might write ~50 versions and the trim engages; a typical session writes far fewer) and large enough to cover a realistic editor session of incremental autosaves without losing the recent edit narrative. The constant lives in `recentVersions.ts` rather than `kv.ts` because it is a per-slug retention policy specific to the versions sorted set, not a KV-client concern; if a future requirement wants a different cap (e.g. higher tier for paid slugs) the slice can override per-slug at the call site without touching the KV namespace. The trim is performed inside the same `try { ... } catch { ... }` block that wraps the version write, the `:latest` write, and the `city:index` write, so a transient KV failure during the trim returns the same 503 the other failures do; the partial state (write succeeded, trim failed) is acceptable because the next successful PUT will trim again. The trim removes only the sorted-set membership; the version payload at `city:${slug}:version:${hash}` is left in place so a stale `?v=<hash>` deep link to a trimmed-from-history snapshot still loads via the existing `loadCity` missing-version branch. Deleting the payload would be a separate (and more dangerous) policy: it would invalidate every share link to that snapshot, which is not what REQ-048 / REQ-049 promise. A future "garbage-collect orphaned payloads" slice could prune payloads whose hashes are no longer in any history sorted set, but that is out of scope for v1; v1's hash space is small (sha256 hex strings, each payload is bounded by `CitySchema`) so the storage cost is acceptable. `MAX_RECENT_VERSIONS_LIMIT === MAX_CITY_VERSIONS` because asking for more entries than the writer keeps is a programming error, not a soft-cap; the read helper enforces the same bound the writer enforces. The `withScores: true` interleaved-result format from Upstash is decoded by walking the array two-at-a-time; this is the documented Upstash shape (`[member, scoreString, member, scoreString, ...]`). The `parseCityVersionHash` filter ensures a future migration that bumps the hash format (e.g. sha512) cannot leak old sha256 entries into a UI that expects the new shape; today both the writer and the parser accept the same `[0-9a-f]{64}` shape so the filter is a defensive belt-and-braces. The new `CityVersionEntry` shape is intentionally `{ hash, updatedAt }` (not `{ hash, timestamp }`) to match the existing PUT response field name (`updatedAt: number`) and the `city:index` writer's `Date.now()` semantics; a future UI can format the timestamp however it likes. Per slice discipline, the version-picker UI itself (a list rendering of `recentVersions` results with click-to-pin links) stays deferred to its own slice; this slice ships only the retention policy, the read helper, and the test surface. The fake-KV `zremrangebyrank` was rewritten because the naive normalize (`Math.max(0, len + idx)`) for negative indices would have made `ZREMRANGEBYRANK 0 -51` on a 2-element set remove the first entry, which is wrong: Redis returns 0 in that case because the resolved range is empty. The corrected resolver computes the raw position first, returns 0 if `rawHi < 0` (the upper bound is below the start of the set) or `rawLo >= len` (the lower bound is past the end), and only then clamps; this matches Redis's actual semantics and was the cause of the first failing test case. `zcard` was added alongside because the trim assertion uses it to verify the set size pre- and post-PUT; both helpers are surgical additions that do not change any other test's behavior.
- GDD coverage: REQ-052 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/03-persistence.md` Status flipped `partial` to `done`; key namespace table updated; Write path step list expanded; Version history reads section added; build log entry recorded.
- Followups: none new. A future "version picker" UI slice (not yet a coverage row) can consume `recentVersions(slug)` and render a list of historical pins linking to `/<slug>?v=<hash>` and `/<slug>/edit?v=<hash>`; the read helper and the bounded history are in place for that work to land cheaply.

## 2026-05-04, Drive Mode Respawn Key: REQ-067

- Branch: `feature/req-067-respawn-key`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed REQ-067 by adding a one-key respawn so a player who tipped off the world, wedged against a building, or simply got turned around can recover without reloading the page. `src/app/[slug]/respawn.ts` ships a pure module with `RESPAWN_KEY_CODE = 'KeyR'` (does not collide with any `DEFAULT_KEY_BINDINGS` action and is not the `Escape` pause key) and `respawnVehicle(pieces)` that calls `spawnAnchor` plus `cellToWorld` plus `rotationToRadians` and returns a fresh `VehicleState` (zero speed) at the spawn anchor's world position with the heading derived from the first piece's rotation; defensive heading 0 when the city has no pieces so the helper is total even though the placeholder car never mounts on an empty grid. `src/app/[slug]/driveHud.ts` `HUD_CONTROLS_HINT_LINES` grew from 5 to 6 rows: a new `R: respawn` row inserted between `D / Right: steer right` and `Esc: pause` so a first-time visitor sees the binding alongside the rest of the vocabulary. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` imports the new module and adds a `handleRespawnKey` window keydown listener installed inside the car-mounted branch after the per-frame helpers `updateVehicleAttrs` / `updateHud` / `updateOnBuildingAttr` / `updateOffStreetAttr` / `applyChaseCamera` / `updateCameraAttrs` are defined so the closure captures the live `let` bindings; the handler short-circuits on the wrong key, on text-target events (mirrors the WASD / Esc guards so a future input on the drive surface keeps `R`-as-text working), on `isPaused(pauseStateRef.current)` so a paused world cannot teleport the car mid-pause, and when no vehicle / car is mounted; otherwise it calls `event.preventDefault()`, reassigns the `let vehicle` binding to `respawnVehicle(city.pieces)`, copies the new pose into `car.position` and `car.rotation`, clears `pressedKeys` (so a held throttle does not relaunch the freshly-spawned car), and refreshes every per-frame mirror (`data-keys-pressed`, `data-car-*`, `data-on-building`, `data-off-street`, `data-hud-*`); when a chase camera rig is mounted it snaps the rig position and target to the new spawn pose via `createCameraRig` so the rig does not lerp in from the wreck position on the next tick. The cleanup branch removes the new keydown listener so navigating away does not leak the binding. `tests/app/respawn.test.ts` ships 13 cases covering the key code (matches `KeyR`, does not collide with `DEFAULT_KEY_BINDINGS`, does not collide with the Escape pause key) and `respawnVehicle` (no-pieces grid origin, first-piece world center, cell-to-world convention, heading from rotation, every cardinal rotation, zero speed, first-piece-only when several placed, negative coords, fresh-object guarantee, no input mutation). `tests/app/driveHud.test.ts` updates the length assertion from 5 to 6 and adds a `mentions R for respawn` case so the controls hint stays in sync with the binding. `e2e/drive.spec.ts` asserts pressing `R` on the empty-state spec is a no-op because the listener gate prevents the handler from firing without a car (`data-vehicle` stays `false`). `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` gained a Respawn key section and a build log entry; the canonical-spec file header lists REQ-067 alongside the rest of the drive-mode requirements.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 603/603 pass (589 prior + 13 new respawn cases + 1 new `mentions R for respawn` case in driveHud.test.ts; the existing `lists every default action plus the pause hint` case in driveHud.test.ts was renamed to `lists every default action plus the respawn and pause hints` and the length assertion bumped from 5 to 6 to match the new `R: respawn` row). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 137 kB unchanged from the prior REQ-066 slice (the new module is tiny and the per-frame mirror updates only add a single function definition). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 18/18 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs with the new `R` no-op assertion + 12 editor specs). `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` validated.
- Assumptions: The respawn key is bound to `KeyR` because `R` is the conventional "stuck" / "reset" key in arcade driving games and is not bound to any existing action in `DEFAULT_KEY_BINDINGS`. The editor's `R`-for-rotate binding lives in a separate page tree (`/<slug>/edit`) so a player who switches between the two surfaces does not see a binding collision. `respawnVehicle` reuses `spawnAnchor` (REQ-036) so the respawn anchor is the same cell the car spawned on at first mount; this is the simplest consistent rule and a player who erased the original first piece sees the anchor shift to whatever the new first piece is, matching the spawn-anchor contract. Heading is derived from the first piece's rotation so the car faces the same direction it spawned in; this matches REQ-036's contract where the persisted rotation flows through to the vehicle group. Speed is zeroed so the integrator does not pick up residual velocity from the wreck; the player can immediately start accelerating from a stop as if they had just loaded the page. The pressed-key set is cleared on respawn so a held throttle does not relaunch the freshly-spawned car at the next frame; this is the inverse of the pause-menu behavior (which intentionally keeps held keys pressed because a paused player wants to resume from the same input) because a respawn is a "reset to spawn" not a "pause and resume" event. The chase camera rig snaps to the new spawn pose via `createCameraRig` so the rig does not lerp in from the wreck position on the next tick; without this snap a player who respawned from a far-off-grid position would see the camera glide across the world for several seconds before catching up. The respawn handler is skipped while the pause menu is open because the integration loop is frozen during pause; respawning while frozen would put the world in an inconsistent state where the car position differs from what the next resumed tick would integrate from. The text-target guard mirrors the WASD / Esc handlers so a future input on the drive surface (e.g. a settings pane) keeps `R`-as-text working; v1 has no such input but the guard keeps the contract consistent. The handler is registered AFTER the per-frame helper functions are defined inside the same `useEffect` so the closure captures the live `let vehicle` binding cleanly; placing it earlier would still work via JavaScript closure semantics (the reference is resolved at call time) but the source order makes the data-flow clearer. The handler is placed in its own `if (car) { ... }` block separate from the WASD / Esc registration block so the change is locally contained at the bottom of the listener-attach section; a future refactor could collapse the two blocks but slice discipline keeps that out of this slice. The HUD controls hint line `R: respawn` sits between `D / Right: steer right` and `Esc: pause` so the binding reads alongside the steering-related bindings (which is when the player is most likely to want it) rather than at the bottom of the list. Per slice discipline, REQ-040 onward (settings pane that lets `R` be rebound), REQ-035 (touch input with a respawn button), and REQ-032 (wheel contact for refined "stuck" detection) all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the keyboard binding and the pose reset.
- GDD coverage: REQ-067 added to `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` as a new row (status `done`). `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` Respawn section added with a build log entry; the canonical-spec file header lists REQ-067 alongside the rest of the drive-mode requirements.
- Followups: none new. F-008 (seed a non-empty city in playwright so visible-movement assertions land) stays open and now has the respawn pose mirror ready to verify alongside the chase camera, the car movement, and the building / off-street penalties once the seed mechanism lands.

## 2026-05-04, URL ?v=<hash> Pins Editor and Drive View: REQ-048, REQ-049

- Branch: `feature/req-048-049-version-pin`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed REQ-048 and REQ-049 by wiring the `?v=<hash>` query parameter through both page routes so a shared link can deep-link into a specific historical version of a city. `src/lib/cityVersion.ts` ships a pure module with `VERSION_HASH_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{64}$/` (sha256 hex digest shape per REQ-013), `parseCityVersionHash(raw)` that returns the branded `CityVersionHash` on a 64-char lowercase hex match and `null` otherwise, and `readVersionParam(raw)` that wraps the `string | string[] | undefined` shape Next.js delivers in `searchParams` (array-shaped values such as `?v=a&v=b` collapse to `null` so a malformed share link cannot silently pick one of the two values). `src/app/api/city/[slug]/route.ts` was migrated off its local `parseVersionHash` helper to the shared `parseCityVersionHash` so the accept / reject contract is identical across the API route and both pages (the local helper was deleted; the import shape is the only call site). `src/app/[slug]/page.tsx` (drive view) and `src/app/[slug]/edit/page.tsx` (editor) now accept `searchParams: Promise<{ v?: string | string[] }>`, validate the `v` field through `readVersionParam`, and call `notFound()` on a malformed pin. A well-formed but unknown hash falls through to `EMPTY_CITY` via `loadCity`'s missing-version branch so a stale share link still renders (the empty-state prompt invites the player to start over). The editor's autosave behavior is unchanged: loading a pinned version and then editing forks (the next PUT advances `:latest` under the slug owner), mirroring VibeRacer's edit-from-history semantics. v1 does not surface a "you are viewing a historical version" banner; the URL is the only signal. `tests/lib/cityVersion.test.ts` ships 21 cases covering the regex (valid 64-hex, all-zero / all-f boundary, empty / too-short / too-long / mixed-case / non-hex / whitespace rejections), `parseCityVersionHash` (valid pass-through and the same rejection set plus path-traversal-style and query-separator rejections), and `readVersionParam` (valid string, undefined, array, empty string, malformed string). `tests/app/editRoute.test.ts` and the new `tests/app/driveRoute.test.ts` cover the page-level `notFound()` throw on too-short / uppercase / empty / array-shaped `?v=` inputs (driveRoute is new this slice; the prior REQ-006 test surface only validated slug shape and never exercised the page route module). `tests/app/cityRoute.test.ts` gained two new GET cases: a pin to a hash whose `:latest` moved on still returns the pinned version, and a well-formed but unknown hash returns `EMPTY_CITY` with `versionHash` null. `docs/gdd/04-slug-routing.md` flipped `Status: partial` to `Status: done`, added a Version pinning section that records the hash format / malformed-hash 404 / unknown-hash empty fallthrough / editor-fork-on-edit semantics, and added a build log entry.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 583/583 pass (551 prior + 21 new cityVersion + 4 new editRoute REQ-048 cases + 10 new driveRoute cases + 2 new cityRoute REQ-048/049 cases minus 2 GET cases that already covered the malformed shape and empty-when-no-pin path; the net is +30). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` and `/[slug]/edit` route-specific JS unchanged (the helper module is small and the page changes add four lines per route, no new dependency). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 18/18 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive + 12 editor specs; the e2e suite covers the success paths via the existing `/some-slug` and `/some-slug/edit` URLs without `?v=`; pinned-version e2e coverage waits for F-008 which seeds a real KV-backed city). `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` validated.
- Assumptions: Malformed `?v=` rejects via `notFound()` (the page route's parallel to the API route's HTTP 400) so a broken share link surfaces 404 instead of silently falling through to the latest version. Silently falling through would be worse: a user who shared a snapshot URL would see the wrong city back without any signal that the pin was lost. The 404 fail-fast surfaces the broken state immediately. A well-formed but unknown hash (a hash whose KV entry is missing because that version was never saved or KV is unconfigured) falls through to `EMPTY_CITY` via `loadCity`'s existing missing-version branch; this preserves the v1 "the empty state is a recoverable state" contract: the page renders, the URL stays meaningful, and the empty-state prompt invites the player to start over. Array-shaped values such as `?v=a&v=b` collapse to `null` (and therefore reject) because picking one of the two would be a guess; the explicit reject is honest about the malformed input. The shared `cityVersion.ts` helper consolidates the hash-shape validation that previously lived only in the API route handler. Splitting it into its own module (rather than inlining the regex into each page) follows the slice discipline rule that three similar lines is better than a premature abstraction; here we have three call sites (route, drive page, editor page) so the helper is the right unit. Loading a pinned version into the editor and then making an edit forks: the next autosave PUT writes a fresh `:latest` pointer under the slug owner. This mirrors VibeRacer's edit-from-history semantics and is the intentional behavior; v1 does not show a "you are viewing a historical version" banner so the URL is the only signal that the editor is opened against a historical pin. Adding the banner is a separate polish slice (a future settings or info-pane surface). The editor's `EditorClient` itself is unchanged: it receives the loaded city via `initialCity` and treats it as the current state; it does not know whether the load came from `:latest` or a pin, and the autosave path does not need to know either. The drive page is similarly unchanged downstream; the only change is the `searchParams` accept and the `loadCity(slug, pinned ?? undefined)` call. The page route's `searchParams` shape (`Promise<{ v?: string | string[] }>`) matches the Next.js 15 App Router convention. The success-path JSX is intentionally not inspected in the unit tests (Vitest runs in node and the drive scene's `<DriveSceneClient>` imports `three` which only runs in a real browser); the playwright e2e suite covers the rendered shell and the new `tests/app/driveRoute.test.ts` covers the page module's pre-JSX rejection contract.
