per-cell `data-cell-occupied-kind`, asserts a building click on the piece cell is rejected (no stacking), asserts a building click on an existing building cell is rejected, switches building type to shop and places another, toggles erase mode, confirms an erase click on the piece cell is a no-op while in building category (erase follows the active category), erases the building, switches back to street category in erase mode and erases the piece, then toggles erase off, switches back to building category, rotates to 90deg, and places a factory at (-2, -2) to confirm rotation flows through to building placement.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 294/294 pass (258 prior + 5 occupiedBuildingCells + 1 occupiedPieceCells exclusion + 4 BUILDING_PALETTE + 2 default constants + 12 placeBuilding + 11 eraseBuilding cases + 1 update to existing snapshot enumerator counts as the same describe block already existed). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` reporting 3.62 kB route-specific JS (up from 3.21 kB to cover the category switcher, the building palette buttons, the second selected-type state, and the building dispatch in the click handler). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 10/10 (smoke + click-to-place + curve palette + rotate + erase + drive CTA + undo / redo + two autosave specs + new building palette spec) in 8.7s. `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of GDD_COVERAGE.json validated.
- Assumptions: One palette + two categories beats two parallel palettes. The category switcher is a `role="tablist"` with two tabs and the palette buttons swap below it, instead of stacking the street palette and the building palette in the same toolbar row. This keeps the click-to-place contract obvious (only one palette is selectable at a time) and avoids the toolbar growing to thirteen buttons in width on a default desktop viewport. Streets is the default category because the build / drive loop turns on a placed road, so a first-time author should be able to place a road without first picking a category. Place and erase both follow the active category. An author in building category + erase mode who clicks a piece cell sees nothing happen, which is the simplest consistent rule (Rule 7 default) and avoids the "did I just lose my road?" surprise that a cross-category erase could create. Building cells render with olive (`#6b7d4a`) versus piece brown (`#7d6b4a`) so the two layers read distinctly on the grid without inventing per-type sprite art. The snap grid's `data-cell-occupied` attribute keeps reporting `true` for both pieces and buildings; the new `data-cell-occupied-kind` attribute is the precise discriminator for tests and future visual layers. v1 buildings are single-cell (Q-004 default B); the `placeBuilding` reducer assumes a single-cell footprint so the overlap check is a single `Set.has` against each occupancy set rather than the multi-cell loop in `placePiece`. Multi-cell building footprints (Q-004 option C) wait until playtest signals visual variety matters. Rotation flows through to building placement so the persisted `rotation` is recorded even though the editor SVG renders buildings as flat colored cells; the future drive-mode render (REQ-046) can orient the extruded building from that value. The piece-count readout grows to include `Buildings placed: N` only when any building is on the grid so the existing "Pieces placed: 0" exact-text assertions in the legacy specs still pass on a city with zero buildings. Building hash semantics: `hashCity` already covers the buildings array (REQ-013), so the new placement / erase paths flow through the existing autosave + version-hash contract without any schema or hash changes. Per slice discipline, REQ-030 (drive-mode collision) stays deferred because the drive scene scaffold (REQ-031) has not landed; the `docs/gdd/08-buildings.md` spec records the collision rule (cell-level off-street penalty, Q-005 default A) as the planned default.
- GDD coverage: REQ-028 flipped `not_started` to `done`. REQ-029 flipped `not_started` to `done`. Created `docs/gdd/08-buildings.md` as the canonical spec for the building requirements (REQ-028 through REQ-030; REQ-030 still deferred). Build log entry added at the top of the new GDD section.
- Followups: none new. REQ-030 (drive-mode collision) stays a not-started row in `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` and is documented in `docs/gdd/08-buildings.md` as the planned cell-level off-street treatment.

## 2026-05-04, Editor Undo and Redo: REQ-023

- Branch: `feature/req-023-undo-redo`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: gave the editor an undo / redo stack. Added `src/app/[slug]/edit/editorHistory.ts`, a port of VibeRacer's `editorHistory.ts` exporting `EditorHistory<T>` (`past` / `present` / `future` arrays), `createHistory(initial)`, `pushHistory(history, next)` with an identity-equality short-circuit so idempotent setters do not pollute the past stack and a `EDITOR_HISTORY_MAX_PAST = 100` cap that drops the oldest entry past the cap, `undoHistory(history)`, `redoHistory(history)`, and the `canUndo` / `canRedo` O(1) flags. Updated `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` so the working city now lives in an `EditorHistory<City>` state seeded from the initial city. The cell-click path routes through a `setCityWithHistory` updater that calls `pushHistory`, so every accepted placement / erase records a new history entry while rejected mutations (overlap, empty-cell erase) stay reference-equal and short-circuit through both `placePiece` / `erasePiece` and `pushHistory`. New `handleUndo` / `handleRedo` callbacks call the matching helpers and flip the autosave status to `pending` when the history actually moved so the resulting post-undo city persists. The toolbar gains an Undo and a Redo button after the Erase button (with `data-testid="editor-undo"` / `editor-redo`, `data-can-undo` / `data-can-redo` attributes mirroring the flags, and `disabled` plus `not-allowed` cursor / 0.5 opacity when the respective stack is empty). The keyboard listener now intercepts modifier-held `Cmd+Z` / `Ctrl+Z` for undo, `Cmd+Shift+Z` / `Ctrl+Shift+Z` for redo, and `Cmd+Y` / `Ctrl+Y` for redo while still skipping text-input targets and still ignoring plain `R` / `E` when a modifier is held (so the rotate / erase shortcuts continue to behave the way REQ-021 / REQ-022 spec). Updated `src/app/[slug]/edit/page.tsx` so the CTA copy mentions the undo / redo shortcuts. Added `tests/app/editorHistory.test.ts` (20 cases: createHistory seeds the present and empty stacks, canUndo / canRedo flags, pushHistory append + identity short-circuit + future clear + cap behavior + immutability, undoHistory walk-back single / multi-step + immutability + no-op when empty, redoHistory replay + walk-forward + immutability + no-op when empty, round-trip with `placePiece` preserving reference equality). Added an `/undo-redo-spec/edit` Playwright spec that places two pieces, asserts both controls start disabled and Undo enables after the first push, undoes via the toolbar button and confirms the second cell unoccupied, redoes via the button, undoes via `Control+z`, redoes via `Control+Shift+z` and `Control+y`, performs a fresh placement after an undo and confirms the redo button disables (the redo stack is cleared by a fresh push), and walks back to the empty city to confirm the undo button disables.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 258/258 pass (238 prior + 20 editorHistory cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` reporting 3.21 kB route-specific JS (up from 2.79 kB to cover the history wrapper, two new toolbar buttons, and the undo / redo keyboard handlers). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 9/9 (smoke + click-to-place + curve palette + rotate + erase + drive CTA + new undo / redo spec + two autosave specs) in 9.7s. `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of GDD_COVERAGE.json validated.
- Assumptions: The history wraps the entire `City`, not just the `pieces` array. This is one extra reference per push but it lets a future building palette slice (REQ-029) flow through the same history without having to widen the wrapped value. The cap matches VibeRacer's `EDITOR_HISTORY_MAX_PAST = 100` so an author who switches between the two projects sees consistent undo depth. Undo / redo flow through autosave (the resulting city is a fresh reference so the debounce kicks in and persists the post-undo state) rather than skipping the network round-trip; this keeps the persistence contract simple: every observable change to the working city is what gets saved. Rotation and tool-mode state (rotate-button angle, erase toggle) live outside the history because they are ergonomic state, not document state. An author who toggles erase, undoes a placement, then continues placing keeps their tool selections; only the city changes. Idempotent setters (clicking an occupied cell in place mode, clicking an empty cell in erase mode) do not pollute the past stack thanks to two layers of identity-equality short-circuit: the reducer returns the original city, and `pushHistory` returns the original history. The keyboard shortcut path explicitly requires a modifier for undo / redo (no plain `Z` or `Y` shortcut), keeping the single-letter keys free for any future text-entry surface in the editor. Both Cmd and Ctrl trigger the shortcut so macOS and Windows / Linux authors get parity without a platform sniff. The undo / redo buttons render as text labels (`Undo` / `Redo`) rather than icons in v1 to match the existing toolbar's text-button vocabulary; an icon variant can land with a future visual polish slice. Undo of the very first placement returns the editor to the originally-loaded city (which is the empty city for a fresh slug, or the saved city for a returning author); we do not separately seed an "initial" snapshot because the history's `present` already starts on that value.
- GDD coverage: REQ-023 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` Build log gained a REQ-023 entry; the Tools section spec text now records the toolbar buttons, the keyboard shortcut bindings, the cap, and the identity-equality short-circuit.
- Followups: none new.

Any visual rotation feedback or sprite art for the curve glyphs lands when REQ-045 (textured piece visuals) does; for now the snap grid renders every placed piece as a plain filled cell regardless of type. Palette ordering puts the S-curve pair before the sweep pair because S-curves are the shape a builder reaches for to add visual interest to a basic loop, and sweeps are the longer-radius variant a builder reaches for after the basic shape is in place; no playtest signal yet so this is the recorded default. The four labels (`S-Curve`, `S-Curve Left`, `Sweep Right`, `Sweep Left`) match VibeRacer's editor naming so a player who builds in both projects sees the same vocabulary. The palette buttons reuse the same styling as the REQ-017 entries; no toolbar wrapping or scrolling treatment is added because seven entries plus Rotate / Erase / Drive still fits the editor toolbar at the v1 grid width on common desktop viewports, and the toolbar already has `flexWrap: wrap` so narrow viewports degrade gracefully.

The CTA renders as a `next/link` rather than a `useRouter().push()` button so the framework handles prefetch automatically and so middle-click / cmd-click open the drive view in a new tab the way a standard nav link does. The CTA stays a single Drive label even in erase mode (we do not gray it out or change copy when erase is active) because the build / drive loop is the editor's primary affordance and a tool-mode change should not alter how the user navigates away. The page-level Drive link is removed rather than kept as a duplicate; per the GDD spec REQ-026 says "Drive CTA in editor toolbar", and slice-discipline says do not ship two ways to do the same thing. ## 2026-05-04, Editor Autosave: REQ-025

- Branch: `feature/req-025-autosave`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed the build / save half of the build-drive loop. Added `src/middleware.ts` (Next.js middleware that issues the `vibecity.builderId` cookie on every page request when the cookie is absent or malformed; `httpOnly` / `sameSite=lax` / `path=/` / one-year `maxAge` matching `BUILDER_ID_COOKIE_MAX_AGE_SEC`; matcher excludes `_next/static` / `_next/image` / `favicon.ico` and any URL with a file extension so static assets do not pay the cookie issuance cost; mirrors VibeRacer's racer-id middleware). Added `src/app/[slug]/edit/autosaveStatus.ts` exporting the `AutosaveStatus = 'idle' | 'pending' | 'saving' | 'saved' | 'error'` union, `DEFAULT_AUTOSAVE_DEBOUNCE_MS = 600`, the `AUTOSAVE_STATUS_LABEL` map (idle / saved share `Saved`, pending = `Editing`, saving = `Saving`, error = `Save failed`), and `isCityContentEqual(a, b)` helper that compares pieces and buildings structurally while excluding `mood` to mirror REQ-013 hash semantics. Updated `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` to accept a `slug` prop, hold an `autosaveStatus` state seeded from `idle`, flip the status to `pending` only when `placePiece` / `erasePiece` returns a fresh city reference (so rejected placements / no-op erases do not trigger a save), and run a debounced autosave effect that issues a single `PUT /api/city/<slug>` per settled streak. The save uses an `AbortController` so a newer streak supersedes any save still in flight; mid-flight edits keep the status `pending` until the next debounce window fires. The render now exposes the indicator via `<p role="status" aria-live="polite" data-testid="editor-autosave-status" data-autosave-status="...">` with the error case rendered in red. Updated `src/app/[slug]/edit/page.tsx` to async-load the city through `loadCity(slug)` (REQ-015 read path) so the editor restores a saved city on reload, falling back to `EMPTY_CITY` when KV is unset; refreshed the CTA copy to read "Edits autosave." Added `tests/app/autosaveStatus.test.ts` (13 cases: enum label coverage, idle / saved label parity, error label, default debounce range, `isCityContentEqual` reference / empty / structural equality / piece count / piece order / piece field / building / mood-only diff). Added `tests/app/middleware.test.ts` (5 cases: mints fresh id when no cookie, sets cookie attributes correctly, preserves a valid existing cookie, overwrites a malformed cookie, overwrites a v1 UUID). Extended `e2e/editor.spec.ts` with two new specs that intercept `**/api/city/**` via `page.route` (so the test does not require KV configured against the Playwright webServer): one asserts the autosave PUT happens after every accepted mutation with the right body shape and that a follow-on placement issues a second PUT once the streak settles; the other forces a 503 response and verifies the status flips to `error` with the `Save failed` label.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 233/233 pass (215 prior + 13 autosaveStatus + 5 middleware). `npm run build` produced a green production build with the middleware now registered (34.6 kB) and `/[slug]/edit` reporting 2.61 kB route-specific JS (up from 2.03 kB to cover the autosave effect, status indicator, and `isCityContentEqual` helper). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 6/6 (smoke + click-to-place + rotate + erase + two new autosave specs) in 9.8s. `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of GDD_COVERAGE.json validated.
- Assumptions: 600 ms is the right default debounce. Short enough that a single placement feels saved (status flips to `Saving` then `Saved` within roughly a second), long enough that a streak of placements only fires one network request once the streak settles. The autosave effect treats the server-loaded initial city as already saved (via `lastSavedCityRef` seeded from `initialCity`) so the editor does not fire an empty-city PUT on first render. Identity equality comparison is structural, not deep cloning; `JSON.stringify` of each piece / building gives a fast deterministic comparison without a recursion helper. Mood is excluded from autosave equality to match REQ-013 hash semantics so a mood-only edit (when REQ-040 lands) will not trigger a save the server would treat as the same version. The middleware cookie is NOT marked `secure` so the local dev server (HTTP) and preview deploys both work; production traffic is HTTPS via the Vercel edge so the cookie still rides on TLS in practice. The middleware match excludes any URL with a `.` character so static asset requests (`/favicon.ico`, `/manifest.json`, etc.) do not pay the cookie set cost on every request. Save failures surface via the indicator (`error` status, red color, "Save failed" label) but do not retry automatically; a future slice can layer exponential-backoff retry on top once playtest signals authors hit the failure mode in real cities. Loading the city via `loadCity` is the read side of REQ-015 (already shipped); this slice makes the read flow visible from the editor route. Per slice discipline, REQ-025 lands with REQ-007 (edit route) flipping `partial` to `done` because the route now fully wires the editor surface end to end (load + edit + save) instead of being a placeholder shell.
- GDD coverage: REQ-025 flipped `not_started` to `done`. REQ-007 flipped `partial` to `done`. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` Build log gained a REQ-025 entry; the Tools section spec text now records the autosave debounce, indicator labels, and load-on-mount behavior.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Editor Erase Tool: REQ-022

- Branch: `feature/req-022-erase-piece`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: gave the editor an erase tool. Added a pure `erasePiece(city, row, col)` reducer to `src/app/[slug]/edit/editorState.ts` that finds the first piece whose resolved footprint covers `(row, col)` and returns a fresh city with that piece removed (or the original city when no piece occupies the cell, so callers can branch on identity equality the same way as `placePiece`). Added a `ToolMode` union (`'place' | 'erase'`) plus `DEFAULT_TOOL_MODE = 'place'` so the editor opens in place mode for first-time authors. Updated `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` to hold a `toolMode` state, render an Erase button in the toolbar (with `data-testid="editor-erase"`, `aria-pressed`, and a `data-tool-mode` attribute that mirrors the active mode; the button's background and label flip from "Erase" / cream to "Erasing" / red when active so the change of intent is visually obvious), wire a `keydown` listener for the `E` key (modifier-aware skip and `INPUT` / `TEXTAREA` / `contentEditable` skip mirroring the rotate shortcut), and route cell clicks to `erasePiece` when in erase mode (otherwise `placePiece` keeps its existing contract). Extended `src/app/[slug]/edit/SnapGridView.tsx` with an optional `cursorMode` prop ('place' | 'erase', default 'place') that swaps the cursor between `crosshair` and `not-allowed` and exposes the active mode as `data-cursor-mode` on the SVG. Updated `src/app/[slug]/edit/page.tsx` so the CTA copy mentions the erase tool and the E shortcut. Extended `tests/app/editorState.test.ts` with 12 new cases (`DEFAULT_TOOL_MODE`, `erasePiece` happy path, no-op identity, immutability, building preservation, multi-cell footprint atomic erase, negative coordinates, deterministic two-pieces-share-a-cell tiebreak, placement-order preservation, place / erase round-trip, schema validity). Extended `e2e/editor.spec.ts` with a third spec that places two pieces, toggles erase mode via the Erase button, asserts cursor / aria / tool-mode attributes flip, clicks an empty cell (no-op) then an occupied cell (piece disappears), toggles back via lowercase / uppercase E, erases the second piece, and confirms Ctrl+E does not toggle. Updated `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` (Status / Tools spec text and Build log) to record REQ-022 as shipped.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 215/215 pass (203 prior + 12 erase cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` reporting 2.03 kB route-specific JS (up from 1.75 kB to cover the erase button, mode state, and E-key listener). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 4/4 (smoke + click-to-place + rotate + new erase spec) in 7.9s. `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of GDD_COVERAGE.json validated.
- Assumptions: Place and erase are mutually exclusive cell-click contracts. The toolbar exposes a single Erase toggle rather than a place-mode button because place is the default and "no Erase pressed" is unambiguously place mode; introducing an explicit Place button would add a chrome element the author rarely interacts with. The `E` keyboard shortcut toggles erase mode (press to enter, press again to exit) rather than holding `E` for an erase-while-held interaction; toggle matches the discoverability of the on-screen button and avoids the polling-needed-to-detect-keyup pattern this slice does not need. Clicking an empty cell in erase mode is a silent no-op; a future slice can add a brief flash if playtest signals authors expect feedback. Multi-cell pieces (mega sweep, hairpin, future arc45 / diagonal per F-002) erase atomically: clicking any cell of the resolved footprint removes the whole piece, matching the placePiece contract that places the whole footprint atomically. The deterministic "first match wins" tiebreak when two pieces share a cell is the only sane behavior for a hand-edited or future-import-corrupted city; placePiece prevents the state in v1, so the case is defensive coverage for the reducer's invariant. Building erase stays out of scope for this slice; it lands with REQ-029 (building palette parity). Rotation is sticky across mode flips so an author who rotates to 90deg, erases a stray piece, and goes back to placing keeps their 90deg rotation. The Erase button uses red (`#a3372a`) when active to signal "destructive mode"; this matches the visual language a first-time author expects for a delete tool without inventing a custom eraser glyph.
- GDD coverage: REQ-022 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` Build log gained a REQ-022 entry; the Tools section spec text was sharpened to mark erase as shipped instead of a future slice.
- Followups: none new.

- Changed: gave the editor a rotate tool. Added pure helpers to `src/app/[slug]/edit/editorState.ts`: `ROTATIONS` (`[0, 90, 180, 270]`, the cycle order), `DEFAULT_ROTATION` (`0`, the canonical orientation), and `nextRotation(current)` (advances one 90deg step, wraps `270 -> 0`). Updated `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` to hold a `rotation` state seeded from `DEFAULT_ROTATION`, render a Rotate button alongside the palette buttons (with `data-testid="editor-rotate"` and a `data-rotation` attribute that mirrors the active angle), wire a `keydown` listener on `window` that cycles rotation when the user presses `R` (modifier-aware so Cmd+R / Ctrl+R browser refresh still works, and ignores key events sourced from `input` / `textarea` / `contentEditable` targets), and pass the active rotation into `placePiece` on every cell click. Extended `e2e/editor.spec.ts` with a second test that walks the rotate button 0 -> 90 -> 180 -> 270 -> 0, verifies lowercase `r` and uppercase `R` keypresses cycle the rotation, places a piece while rotated, and confirms Ctrl+R does not hijack rotation. Updated `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` (Piece palette spec text and Build log) to record REQ-021 as shipped.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 203/203 pass (191 prior + 12 rotation cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` reporting 1.75 kB route-specific JS (up from 1.44 kB to cover the rotate handler and key listener). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 3/3 (smoke + click-to-place + new rotation spec) in 6.8s. `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of GDD_COVERAGE.json validated.
- Assumptions: Rotation cycles forward only in v1 (no Shift+R reverse cycle); a power user who rotates past their target can hit `R` three more times without paying enough cost to justify a second binding in this slice. The Rotate button lives inside the same toolbar `role="toolbar"` as the palette buttons rather than a separate toolbar so the editor surface stays a single visual cluster; if the editor grows a third tool group (erase, undo) the toolbar will split out then. The `R` keyboard shortcut listens on `window` rather than a focused element so the user does not have to first focus the grid to rotate; the modifier-aware skip and the `INPUT` / `TEXTAREA` / `contentEditable` skip cover the only cases where a global key listener would be a misfeature in v1. Rotation does not yet rotate the piece glyph in the SVG render because the snap grid renders piece footprints as plain filled cells, not oriented sprites; the visual rotation feedback lands when REQ-045 (textured piece visuals) does. Switching the selected palette entry does NOT reset the rotation; rotation is sticky across selection changes so an author who rotates left90 to 90deg and then switches to right90 keeps the 90deg orientation. This matches "the editor is a tool, not a wizard" and avoids a surprise reset on every palette click.
- GDD coverage: REQ-021 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` Build log gained a REQ-021 entry; the Piece palette spec text was sharpened to mark rotation as shipped instead of a future slice.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Editor Palette and Click-to-Place: REQ-017, REQ-020

Added `src/app/[slug]/edit/editorState.ts` exporting `STREET_PALETTE` (cardinal-only v1: `straight`, `left90`, `right90` per REQ-017), `DEFAULT_PALETTE_TYPE` (selects `straight` on first render), and a pure `placePiece(city, type, row, col, rotation?)` reducer that appends the new piece or returns the original city on footprint overlap so the click handler can rely on identity equality. Added `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx`, a `'use client'` component holding the selected piece type and the working city, rendering palette buttons with `aria-pressed` / `data-piece-type` plus a piece-count readout, and dispatching `placePiece` from the grid click handler. Extended `src/app/[slug]/edit/SnapGridView.tsx` with an optional `onCellClick(row, col)` prop that turns each cell `<rect>` into a clickable target and flips the cursor to crosshair. Updated `src/app/[slug]/edit/page.tsx` to render `<EditorClient initialCity={EMPTY_CITY} />` (replacing the static `<SnapGrid />`) and rewrote the call-to-action paragraph to read "Pick a piece, click the grid to place it." Added `tests/app/editorState.test.ts` (17 cases covering palette enumeration, labels, schema-validity, default selection, append semantics, immutability, negative coordinates, rotation default, building preservation, and footprint overlap rejection across single / multi-cell pieces). Added `e2e/editor.spec.ts` (Playwright spec that visits `/playtest-city/edit`, walks each palette entry, places three pieces, asserts the visible piece count plus the SVG `data-occupied-count` attribute, and confirms duplicate clicks on an occupied cell are no-ops).

JSON syntax of GDD_COVERAGE.json validated.

Selected-piece state lives in the EditorClient component (React `useState`), not in a Redux-style store, because v1 only needs single-component state. The placePiece reducer rejects overlapping placement (REQ-027) by returning the original city; the UI does not yet flash a rejection animation, just a no-op. Rotation is hard-coded to `0` for the click-to-place handler in this slice; the rotation tool (REQ-021) lands in its own slice and will let the user pick rotation before placement. The editor still seeds with `EMPTY_CITY` directly; loading saved cities (REQ-015) into the editor lands with autosave (REQ-025) so read and write paths can ship together. The Playwright spec uses a fixed slug `playtest-city`; persistence is not yet wired so this does not write to KV.

Added `tests/app/snapGrid.test.ts` with 22 cases covering constant invariants, `cellKey` shape, `gridCells` enumeration / order / origin uniqueness, `cellToPixel` anchor cases (top-left, center, bottom-right), `pieceFootprintCells` single / explicit / multi-cell expansion, and `occupiedPieceCells` empty / aggregate / dedupe semantics. Cell pixel size is 32 px, the smallest comfortable touch target without forcing horizontal scroll on common viewports. Coordinate convention mirrors VibeRacer: row indices grow downward, column indices grow rightward, negative cells are legitimate. The grid renders as SVG rather than a `<canvas>` so the React server component can emit static markup without a client runtime; the place / rotate / erase tools (REQ-020 onward) will add their own client surface when they land. The grid only renders pieces, not buildings, this slice; v1 buildings are single-cell so a future slice that wires building placement can extend `occupiedPieceCells` symmetrically. The `data-cell-row` / `data-cell-col` / `data-cell-occupied` attributes on each `<rect>` are deliberate hooks for the future click-to-place E2E smoke (REQ-020). The editor route still seeds with `EMPTY_CITY` directly; loading a saved city into the editor lands with REQ-025 (autosave) so the read and write paths can ship together.

## 2026-05-03, Playwright E2E Smoke: REQ-003

- Branch: `feature/20260503-175745-req-003-playwright`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: wired the Playwright end-to-end runner. Added `@playwright/test ^1.59.1` to devDependencies. New `playwright.config.ts` (chromium-only project, `testDir: 'e2e'`, `webServer` boots `npm run build && npm run start -- --port 3100` on a non-default port so a local dev server can keep using 3000, `reuseExistingServer` enabled outside CI, `retries: 2` and single worker on CI). New `e2e/smoke.spec.ts` asserts that GET `/` returns 200 and renders the `VibeCity` `<h1>` plus the city builder pitch text. Added `npm run test:e2e` and `npm run test:e2e:install` scripts. Updated `tsconfig.json` to include `e2e/**/*.ts` and `playwright.config.ts` so `tsc --noEmit` validates the e2e tree. Added `e2e/AGENTS.md` symlink to slice-discipline so Codex picks up the rule under the new directory. Added an `e2e-tests` job to `.github/workflows/ci.yml` that installs the chromium browser via `npx playwright install --with-deps chromium`, runs `npm run test:e2e`, and uploads the HTML report as an artifact on every run. Updated `docs/gdd/02-tech-stack.md` to flip REQ-003 to done with a Build log entry, and refreshed the README dev script section.
- Verification: `npx playwright install chromium` populated the local browser cache. `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 152/152 pass (unchanged; e2e tree is excluded from vitest by `tests/**` include scope). `npm run build` produced a green production build. `npx playwright test` passed 1/1 in 7.4s. `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of GDD_COVERAGE.json validated.
- Assumptions: Playwright tests live under `e2e/` (separate from `tests/`) so vitest's `tests/**` include never accidentally tries to run a Playwright spec. The webServer command runs `npm run build` before `npm run start` to mirror production; that adds latency to the local dev loop but matches what CI runs and avoids the dev-only HMR / strict-mode double-render edge cases. Port 3100 is the default to avoid colliding with a developer's `npm run dev` on 3000. The chromium-only project keeps CI runtime small; firefox / webkit projects are deferred until the route surface justifies the cost. The `webServer` block uses `reuseExistingServer: !process.env.CI` so a developer who already has `npm run start -- --port 3100` in another terminal does not pay the build / start cost on every test run. The Playwright HTML report is uploaded as an artifact only; it is not gated, so a CI failure still surfaces in the GitHub UI before the artifact step runs. Branch protection updates to require the new `e2e-tests` job are owner-side configuration and out of scope for this slice.
- GDD coverage: REQ-003 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/02-tech-stack.md` Build log gained an entry for REQ-003.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-03, City Save and Load API: REQ-014, REQ-015

- Branch: `feature/req-014-015-city-api`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: shipped the round-trip persistence path. Added `src/app/api/city/[slug]/route.ts` with GET (reads latest, or `?v=<hash>` pinned read for REQ-048 / REQ-049 deep links) and PUT (validates slug, validates `vibecity.builderId` cookie, parses body against `CitySchema`, computes `hashCity()`, writes `version:${hash}` then `:latest` then ZADDs `versions` and `city:index`, then on first PUT claims `:owner`). Added `src/lib/loadCity.ts` (`loadCity(slug, version?)`) so any caller (route handler, server component) can ask for "the saved city or an empty one" without each branch re-implementing the soft-fallback path. Extended `kvKeys` with `cityOwner(slug)` so the new ownership key is namespaced alongside the rest of the persistence keys. Added `tests/_fakeKv.ts` (in-memory KV port from VibeRacer's `tests/unit/_fakeKv.ts`; covers `set`, `get`, `del`, `zadd`, `zrange`, `zscore`, `lpush`, `lrange`, `ltrim`). Added `tests/lib/loadCity.test.ts` (7 cases) and `tests/app/cityRoute.test.ts` (12 cases). Updated `docs/gdd/03-persistence.md` Key namespace table to document `cityOwner`, sharpened the Write path step list to spell out the first-claim ownership semantics, and appended a Build log entry. Updated `tests/lib/kv.test.ts` with two new cases for the `cityOwner` key.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 152/152 pass (132 prior + 7 loadCity + 12 cityRoute + 1 cityOwner key + lifted total). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/api/city/[slug]` registered as a dynamic server-rendered route. `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of GDD_COVERAGE.json validated.
- Assumptions: Ownership is "first PUT wins": the slug is unowned until a PUT lands, then the requesting builder id is recorded under `city:${slug}:owner`. Subsequent PUTs whose cookie does not match return 403 with `error: "not owner"`. This is a soft enforcement: a builder who clears the cookie loses edit rights to their slug, by design (matches REQ-009 v1 trade-off). Writes are sequenced, not `Promise.all`-ed, so the version key always exists before `:latest` advances and a concurrent reader can never see a `:latest` pointing at a missing version. Hash format on `?v=` is constrained to 64 lowercase hex chars (sha256 hex shape from REQ-013) at the route layer; loadCity does not re-validate the shape because the route is the only public caller today and `loadCity` itself trusts internal callers (slice-discipline). Stored payload corruption (an `:latest` whose pointed-at version key fails `CitySchema`) is treated as a soft empty-city fallback with a `console.warn`, not a 500. The route does not yet trim `versions` to a bounded window; that lives with REQ-052.
- GDD coverage: REQ-014 flipped `not_started` to `done`. REQ-015 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/03-persistence.md` Build log gained a REQ-014 + REQ-015 entry; the section status stays `partial` because REQ-052 (versions trim policy) and REQ-011 / REQ-050 (home-page index reads) remain in scope.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-03, Em-Dash CI Check: REQ-051

- Branch: `feature/req-051-em-dash-ci`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: enforced AGENTS.md Rule 1 in CI. Added `scripts/check-no-dashes.sh` (portable bash + perl scanner that reads tracked files via `git ls-files` and fails with a non-zero exit when U+2014 EM DASH or U+2013 EN DASH appears). Added `.github/workflows/ci.yml` with four jobs (`dash-check`, `type-check`, `unit-tests`, `build`) so every push to main and every PR runs the dash check, `tsc --noEmit`, vitest, and `next build`. Added `npm run check:dashes` so the same check runs locally with one command. Added `tests/scripts/checkNoDashes.test.ts` with 5 cases that drive the script against ephemeral git repos (em-dash fixture rejects, en-dash fixture rejects, hyphens-only fixture passes, gitignored node_modules is skipped, the live VibeCity tree is currently clean). Added `scripts/AGENTS.md` symlink to slice-discipline so Codex picks up the rule under the new directory.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 132/132 pass (127 prior + 5 dash-check cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build. `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of GDD_COVERAGE.json validated.
- Assumptions: The script targets tracked files via `git ls-files` rather than walking the working tree with `--exclude-dir=node_modules`. This is intentional: it inherits gitignore for free and skips build output (`.next`, `dist`, `coverage`) without per-directory excludes, which matters because Next.js writes em-dashes into its own minified runtime. Perl is available on every Ubuntu runner image and on macOS by default, so the script does not depend on GNU grep's `-P` (BSD grep on macOS rejects it). The workflow uses Node 20 to match Vercel's default Next.js 15 runtime; bumping to a newer LTS is a separate concern. The new CI workflow does not gate merging by default until repo branch protection is configured; that is owner-side configuration and out of scope for this slice.
- GDD coverage: REQ-051 flipped `not_started` to `done`. AGENTS.md Rule 1 has not been a GDD section file (the `gddRef` is `AGENTS.md#rule-1`); no GDD section build log entry needed.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-03, City Hash: REQ-013

- Branch: `feature/req-013-city-hash`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: added `src/lib/hashCity.ts` exporting `hashCity(city)` (sha256 hex digest branded as `CityVersionHash`) and `canonicalCityJson(city)` (the deterministic JSON form fed to the digest). - Assumptions: Hash format is sha256 hex 64-char (matches VibeRacer's `hashTrack` output shape so external tooling can treat both projects' version hashes the same way). v1 footprint normalization handles dedupe / `-0` collapse / sort but not rotation symmetry: two pieces that resolve to the same shape under different rotations still hash differently because `rotation` is part of the canonical form (matches VibeRacer). The `Pick<City, 'pieces' | 'buildings'>` parameter shape lets callers pass partial inputs (e.g. an editor's working state) without first constructing a full `City` value. `branchEdges` and `checkpoints` from VibeRacer's `hashTrack` are intentionally not ported: VibeCity does not have lap timing, and branch edges land in the REQ-064 segment-based path slice if needed.
- GDD coverage: REQ-013 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/06-city-schema.md` Build log gained an entry for REQ-013.
- Followups: none new.
